« Le jour est venu de rendre le numéro 7 » : Cristiano Ronaldo annonce sa retraite internationale !

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ce jeudi 5 novembre 2026, Cristiano Ronaldo a officialisé sa retraite internationale avec le Portugal à travers une vidéo d'environ cinq minutes et une lettre d'adieu publiées sur ses réseaux sociaux. Âgé de 41 ans, l'attaquant met un terme à une aventure de 23 ans débutée en août 2003 avec la Seleção das Quinas.

Dans sa lettre adressée aux supporters portugais, Cristiano Ronaldo a expliqué que le jour était venu de rendre le maillot numéro 7 et de le transmettre aux générations futures, précisant qu'il s'agissait d'un choix dicté tant par le cœur que par la tête. L'attaquant a également rendu hommage à ses coéquipiers, à ses entraîneurs, à l'ensemble du staff technique et médical, ainsi qu'à sa famille et aux supporters portugais à travers le monde. La fin d'une ère pour la sélection.

Au-delà de ces explications, le joueur a tenu à saluer la ferveur constante du public qui l'a accompagné pendant plus de deux décennies. L'annonce de son départ a d'ailleurs immédiatement suscité l'émotion de son ancien coéquipier Pepe. Ce dernier a ainsi tenu à saluer son parcours sur les réseaux sociaux en commentant la publication : « C'était bien plus que du football. Merci pour tout, Cris ».

Cette annonce vient clore une aventure débutée en août 2003 avec la Seleção das Quinas. En 23 ans de carrière en sélection, Cristiano Ronaldo s'est constitué un bilan sans précédent sur la scène internationale. Il détient en effet le record mondial masculin de sélections et de buts en équipe nationale avec 146 réalisations en 234 matches. Un bilan exceptionnel. Au cours de cette épopée de 23 ans sous le maillot portugais, l'attaquant a marqué l'histoire de son pays.

Avec la sélection portugaise, Cristiano Ronaldo a ainsi remporté l'Euro 2016 ainsi que deux Ligues des nations (2018-2019 et 2024-2025). Toutefois, cette retraite ne concerne que la scène internationale. En effet, le joueur de 41 ans ne met pas un terme à sa carrière sur les terrains et poursuit son parcours en club avec Al-Nassr.

Cette annonce survient dans la foulée de tensions survenues lors du rassemblement de septembre 2026. L'attaquant avait alors quitté la sélection après avoir passé l'intégralité du match sur le banc sans entrer en jeu contre la Norvège sous les ordres de Jorge Jesus. Malgré cet épisode houleux et une sanction de 30 jours purgée, Cristiano Ronaldo avait tout de même reçu une pré-convocation pour le rassemblement de novembre avant de choisir de raccrocher définitivement en sélection.

D'ailleurs, la Fédération portugaise de football a publié un communiqué pour saluer son capitaine emblématique et a annoncé qu'un hommage officiel digne de son immense carrière lui serait prochainement rendu. Désormais, l'équipe nationale doit se projeter sur la suite de la compétition. Le Portugal disputera sa prochaine rencontre officielle le samedi 14 novembre 2026 à 20h45 face au Danemark à l'Estádio Municipal de Braga en Ligue des nations.

Mercato - Ça bouge à l’OM : Qui doit être le prochain joueur à partir cet été ?

Generali Stadium: Inter e Milan insieme conviene? La risposta. Ricavi? Altro che 60 mln: cifre choc!

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Il nuovo Generali Stadium di Inter e Milan potrebbe cambiare radicalmente la capacità dei due club di generare ricavi e competere economicamente con le big europee.

È quanto emerge dall’analisi aggiornata di Football Benchmark, firmata dal CEO Andrea Sartori, che prova a rispondere a tre domande fondamentali: quanto può rendere il nuovo stadio, quanto costerà e perché continuare a condividerlo può essere la scelta più conveniente per Inter e Milan.

Il progetto ha compiuto un passo decisivo nel novembre 2025, quando i due club hanno completato l’acquisto di San Siro e delle aree circostanti dal Comune di Milano.

Il 29 ottobre 2026 è stato poi presentato il progetto del nuovo Generali Stadium, impianto da circa 71.500 posti destinato a sostituire l’attuale Meazza. L’inizio dei lavori è previsto nel 2028, mentre la prima partita dovrebbe essere disputata nella stagione 2031/32.

The Mag Podcast Episode 14: Everton misery, Newcastle’s transfer failings and the Blame game

Published: 4/11/2026 | Duration: 54 mins | Hosts: Jamie Smith, Simon Cahill & David Hutchinson

In Episode 14 of The Mag Pod, we find Jamie, Simon, and Hutch discussing their experience of Everton misery (again!). Who or what do you point the finger at for how things are now looking at St James’ Park? Are there any answers, or just endless additional questions about the direction the club is heading in?

You can play episode 14 on the audio player above or use your favourite podcast platforms including Spotify, Apple Podcasts, Amazon Music, YouTube, and Acast.

Listen to the latest episode of The Mag Pod! Join Jamie, Simon, and Hutch for unfiltered football debate.

Newcastle United’s miserable run against Everton, growing relegation fears and questions over the club’s transfer spending dominate this week’s Mag Pod.

Jamie Smith is joined by Simon Cahill and David Hutchinson to pick through another dismal night at St James’ Park, the disappointment of exiting the Carabao Cup and the increasingly uncomfortable debate over who is responsible for Newcastle’s dramatic decline.

The lads begin with Monday night’s 1-0 home defeat to Everton, a performance that left supporters frustrated long before the final whistle. With misplaced passes, little attacking threat and a tactical system that appears to be causing more problems than it solves, there’s plenty to discuss about what has gone wrong on the pitch.

The conversation also turns to the League Cup exit and whether losing that familiar source of hope has made Newcastle’s Premier League struggles feel even worse. With no European football to provide a distraction, just how worried should supporters be about the possibility of relegation?

Then comes the blame game. Jamie brings some uncomfortable figures to the table concerning Newcastle’s recruitment, including £547 million spent on 14 signings since May 2025, with only four of those players starting against Everton. The panel debates the quality of the recruitment, the contribution of established players and whether the current manager is getting enough from the squad at his disposal.

There’s also a discussion about the opportunities being given to younger players, the growing pressure from the stands and the possibility of another managerial change. Could Rafa Benitez really be an option again, or would that simply create another set of problems?

Finally, attention turns to the upcoming trip to Fulham and whether Newcastle can find a much-needed response.

It’s a frustrated, occasionally heated and typically humorous Mag Pod, with three supporters trying to make sense of a season that’s heading in the wrong direction.

43 runs over a century! Virat Kohli's unexpected choice for his best ODI innings of all time

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Virat Kohli has made an unexpected choice while reflecting on his finest ODI innings, placing a 43-run knock against England in the 2013 ICC Champions Trophy final above several of his more celebrated centuries in the format.The former India captain, who turned 38 on Thursday, was featured in a video shared by Star Sports in which he was asked to pick his greatest ODI innings. The interaction came a day after he produced an impressive 74 off just 51 balls against New Zealand.

Kohli's selections threw up a few surprises, with the batting great repeatedly favouring innings that might not immediately come to mind when recalling his most memorable performances.His first choice was his 35-run contribution in the 2011 ODI World Cup final against Sri Lanka. He preferred that knock over his unbeaten 133 against Sri Lanka in Hobart in 2012, when India chased down a target of 321 in just 40 overs. He also overlooked his 85 against Australia in the 2023 World Cup opener, an innings played when India were struggling at 2/3 while chasing 199.Kohli continued to back his World Cup final contribution when it was put up against his 122-run effort against England in 2017. That century came during a challenging chase of 351, with India reduced to 63/4. His unbeaten 160 against South Africa in Cape Town in 2018, where he scored 100 runs through running between the wickets, also failed to make the cut.However, when asked to choose between his 35 in the 2011 World Cup final and his performance in the 2013 Champions Trophy final against England, Kohli eventually picked the latter.The 43-run innings came in a rain-affected contest reduced to 20 overs, with India in trouble at 66/5. Kohli joined forces with Ravindra Jadeja to put together a valuable 47-run partnership, while Jadeja contributed an important 33. India eventually clinched the title with a five-run victory.Kohli's final selection also came at the expense of some outstanding centuries. These included his 183 against Pakistan in the 2012 Asia Cup while chasing 330, his 138 against South Africa in Chennai in 2015 and his 107 against Pakistan in the 2015 ODI World Cup. The latter was particularly significant as it made him the first Indian batter to score a World Cup century against Pakistan.

Kohli has built an extraordinary collection of achievements during his international career. His trophy cabinet includes an ODI World Cup, a T20 World Cup, two Champions Trophy titles and two Indian Premier League (IPL) trophies. He also holds the distinction of being India's most successful Test captain, having guided the team to 40 victories in 68 matches, with 17 defeats.In Test cricket, Kohli has accumulated 9,230 runs in 123 matches at an average of 46.85. His tally includes 30 centuries and 31 half-centuries, making him India's fourth-highest run-scorer in the format.His numbers in ODIs are equally remarkable. Kohli is the second-highest run-getter in the history of the format, with 15,183 runs from 318 matches and 306 innings at an average of 58.84. He has registered 55 centuries, a record in ODI cricket, along with 80 fifties.In T20 Internationals, he has scored 4,188 runs in 125 innings at an average of 48.69, including one century and 38 half-centuries. He ranks third on the all-time run-scoring list in the format.Across all three formats, Kohli has amassed 28,601 international runs in 566 matches and 633 innings at an average of 52.86. His overall tally comprises 86 centuries and 149 half-centuries, with his highest individual score being an unbeaten 254.

Bromley appoint ex-MK Dons & Salford boss Robinson

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

League One's bottom side Bromley have named former MK Dons and Oxford United boss Karl Robinson as their new manager.

Robinson, 46, who has been out of work since leaving Salford City in June, has signed a "long-term contract".

Byron Webster, who took interim charge following the sacking of Andy Woodman last month, will stay at Bromley as part of Robinson's coaching staff.

The Ravens are three points from the safety line in League One after winning promotion to the third tier for the first time in their history last season.

"Karl brings an elite level of experience, tactical acumen, and a proven track record of competing at the sharp end of League One," chairman Robin Stanton-Gleaves told the club website.

"His relentless energy matches our ambition perfectly, and we are unified in our vision to drive this football club forward into an exciting new era.

"I led a full recruitment process, which began the day after our previous manager's departure. From the outset, my intention was to find someone who had worked at this level and had the experience and qualities to take Bromley Football Club on the next stage of its journey.

"It was important to take the time to work through that process thoroughly, and I am delighted that it has brought us to Karl."

Robinson's first game in charge will be Saturday's FA Cup first-round meeting with Rotherham United, who are managed by his former number two at Salford, Alex Bruce.

Woodman, who was dismissed on 22 October, guided the club to two promotions in three seasons but left after two wins in the first 10 games this term.

Robinson spent more than six years in charge of MK Dons, which included a 4-0 League Cup win over Manchester United in October 2014, and promotion to the Championship seven months later after finishing runners-up in League One.

The team suffered immediate relegation and he was sacked by the Dons in October 2016.

Robinson then spent 16 months in charge of Charlton Athletic, departing by mutual consent in March 2018 and immediately being named the new Oxford United boss.

He twice took the U's to the League One play-offs, before losing his job in February 2023.

After a spell as assistant manager at Leeds United, Robinson took over at Salford City in January 2024, and last season led the club to their highest ever finish, of fourth in League Two.

However, they lost the play-off final to Notts County and Robinson was surprisingly sacked.

Dave Challinor confirms Championship job desire and rival interest before Sheffield Wednesday swoop

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sheffield Wednesday officially announced Dave Challinor as their new boss earlier this week. The move marked the 51-year-old’s first job since leaving Stockport County in the summer, but his success with the Hatters meant SWFC weren’t alone in eyeing him.

Speaking to The Star, Challinor revealed other clubs wanted ‘conversations’, and his eyes were initially on a step up to the Championship before joining the Owls – a club with ambitions of returning to the second tier. He said: “Without going into too much detail, obviously people wanted to have conversations. I was in the fortunate position where I didn’t need to jump back in. I came out of a really good football club, and we had success, so I had the luxury of being able to be a little bit selective and wait.

“My natural progression would have been to try and get a Championship opportunity. That made the number of clubs I am appealing to was probably pretty small. The next opportunity either needed to give me that chance, or be one I could grow with. I certainly couldn’t have picked a bigger one in terms of this club and the amount of growth it’s got in it.”

Challinor was more than justified in initially eyeing a step up to the Championship after his Stockport County departure. He took the Hatters from the National League to the brink of promotion from League One, but there was a mutual agreement that it was a good time to part ways.

His back-to-back top six finishes in League One and promotion pedigree made him the ideal candidate for Sheffield Wednesday. And, if the Chester-born boss wasn’t going to land a Championship job, moving to a third-tier side with promotion ambitions made for the next best option.

Arguably though, the SWFC job is more exciting than many in the Championship. The ongoing rebuild under new ownership and desire to wake this sleeping giant makes the managerial post a highly attractive one, so Challinor is progressing in job stature despite staying in the same league.

Challinor is a coup for the Owls, and it’s an exciting step for him too. It’s a win-win appointment that benefits all parties, as long as results follow.

Challinor watched on from the stands as Sheffield Wednesday played out a 1-1 draw with Blackpool under Matt Hamshaw last week. The new boss has been able to get to work with his squad this week though, and the first game is just around the corner.

SWFC face League Two side Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday. It marks a favourable start for Challinor, both in terms of the level of the opposition and the fact that there aren’t points at stake.

David Storch has been eager to stress that this appointment is part of a long-term rebuild, and success will not be defined by how this season goes. Nevertheless, Challinor will be determined to get Wednesday firing as soon as he can to strengthen their case for a spot in League One’s top six at the very least.

Cristiano Ronaldo statement in full after stunning retirement decision

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo confirmed that he would retire from international football in a shock announcement on Tuesday.

The 41-year-old calls time on his illustrious 23-year career playing for his country, where he scored 146 goals in 234 games, becoming the all-time leading scorer in the history of men’s international football.

He lifted the European Championships in 2016, and he also won two Nations League titles in 2019 and 2025.

At last summer’s World Cup, the striker could not stop his side crashing out in the last 16 against Spain, when Mikel Merino netted a last-minute winner.

Now, the curtain has come down on his international journey, just weeks after his historic rival Lionel Messi did the same with Argentina.

He had been suspended by the Portuguese FA last month for 30 days, as well as receiving a £215 fine, after storming out of the Nations League camp having fallen out with manager Jorge Jesus.

In an emotional five-minute video posted to his social media channels, Ronaldo addressed the Portuguese people.

"To the Portuguese people, the day has come to hand back the shirt and the captain's armband of the national team that belongs to all of us,” he began.

"I never felt that it was exclusively mine, but for 23 years, I had the privilege and responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me to stadiums all around the world.

"Today, the moment has come to pass it on to the future.

"It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the head alone. They are also made with the heart.

Ronaldo makes bombshell retirement decision after Portugal suspension

Glazer risks Man Utd fan fury with bold claim in rare public interview

Transfer news LIVE: Shock Arsenal move, Kroupi twist; Man Utd stunner

Discover a hidden island paradise in the Indian Ocean

"I am bringing to an end a journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever.

"When I look back and think about everything I experienced with the national team, I don't just see matches, goals, records, or trophies. I see people.

"I see immigrants who, for 90 minutes, felt a little closer to Portugal. I see millions of Portuguese people whom I never met personally, but who became part of my life.

"Because, at the end of the day, football is much bigger than a game. Football is about people.

"Throughout my entire career with the national team, I always tried to push beyond my limits. I wanted to win, I wanted to be the best, I wanted to make history.

"I was always demanding of myself. I always wanted more. I always believed we could go further, and I gave everything I had for this shirt.

"There were perfect afternoons and nights. There were victories I will never forget. There were defeats that still hurt to remember today.

"I also failed. I also suffered. There were moments when I couldn't give the Portuguese people what they wanted so badly. But I never gave up.

"And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

"Over these years, I learned some very simple things: goals come and go, trophies end up in museums, records are eventually broken, but there is something that time can never erase.

"If I ever made you happy, if one of my goals ever made you hug someone, forget a worry, or feel proud of our country, then it was all worth it.

"Because being Portuguese is also about that.

"We suffer together. We get back up together. We believe again.

"Portugal will always need young people who dream, players who never give up, and new generations who believe it is possible to reach places no one has reached before.

"Because a country that continues to create new heroes is a country that never stops believing in itself.

"To my national team teammates, I want to leave a special message.

"Thank you for every run, every hug, every argument, every moment of silence before stepping onto the pitch, every victory we celebrated together, and every defeat that made us stronger.

"I had the privilege of being your captain, but no captain walks alone, and you are all part of my story.

"To all the coaches, directors, doctors, physiotherapists, kit managers, staff members, and the many people who worked with me throughout these years, thank you.

"Many of you never appeared in the photographs, but without you, none of those photographs would have been possible.

"To my family, thank you for sharing this dream with me.

"Thank you for understanding the absences, the sacrifices, and the moments when the national team had to take up a space that also belonged to you.

"If I made it this far, it was because we always walked hand in hand.

"And to the Portuguese people, to every Portuguese person around the world, I want to tell you one last thing.

"There is an image that I will always carry with me.

"A moment when the national anthem begins.

"I look around me. I see my teammates. I see the stands.

"I see children, mothers, fathers, and grandparents. I see Portuguese people who travelled from so far away just to be there.

"And during those moments, we stop being different.

"There are no clubs. There are no colours. There are no classes. There are no professions. There are no opinions.

"That is the feeling I will carry with me for the rest of my life.

"Today, my journey on the pitch with the national team comes to an end.

"I leave with my head held high, proud of the journey I have taken and knowing that I gave everything I had for Portugal.

"I will continue to suffer, to feel the excitement, and to celebrate every Portuguese victory, just as I always have.

"I spent my life working to become the best player in the world.

"Today, I realise that my greatest victory was earning the honour of representing the best country in the world.

"Thank you for allowing me to live a dream that began when I was just a little boy in Madeira, running after a ball.

"The number 7 shirt remains, but the pride of having worn it will be mine forever.

"Portugal will always be a part of me. Forever."

Cristiano Ronaldo has announced his decision to retire from international duty with Portugal after a legendary 23 years representing his country.

The 41-year-old Al Nassr forward recently had a falling-out with Portugal manager Jorge Jesus as he left the national team camp after being an unused substitute in a game against Norway.

It now seems Ronaldo has kicked his last ball for Portugal, and his retirement from international football follows the recent decision from Lionel Messi to do the same.

It truly is the end of an era as these two greats will now no longer play international football, while their retirement from football overall surely can’t be too far away either.

“Today the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the mind alone – they are also made with the heart.

“A journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end.”

Ronaldo ends his Portugal career with a stunning 146 goals in 324 caps, whilst leading his country to glory at Euro 2016.

Ronaldo is still chasing 1000 career goals, and in all likelihood he will get there pretty soon.

The veteran forward is now on 985 total career goals, and he remains a star performer in the Saudi Pro League, so shouldn’t have too much trouble making it to that remarkable milestone that he’s coveted so much.

Ronaldo will turn 42 in February, though, so we are surely now witnessing the end of his incredible playing career, during which he’s established himself as one of the very best players of all time.

Messi is also now 39 and still going strong in the MLS with Inter Miami, but it will be interesting to see how much longer he can carry on too, and if he’ll have a realistic shot at matching Ronaldo’s overall career goal record.

‘I leave with my head held high’ – Ronaldo confirms Portugal retirement

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Portugal icon Cristiano Ronaldo has announced his retirement from international football.

Ronaldo is the leading scorer and appearance-maker in the history of men's internationals, plundering 146 goals in 234 games since his Portugal debut in 2003.

He captained Portugal to their first-ever major trophy at Euro 2016, with Eder scoring the winning goal against hosts France in the final after he had gone off injured, while also lifting the Nations League trophy on two occasions, in 2019 and 2025.

Ronaldo considered retiring from Selecao duty after their round-of-16 exit at this year's World Cup, only to be talked into staying on by the FPF (Portuguese Football Federation).

However, Ronaldo then abruptly left their camp during the last international break, accusing new head coach Jorge Jesus of breaching his trust after he was left on the bench throughout a Nations League win over Norway.

Ronaldo, who also accused Jesus of failing to read a series of pre-agreed points regarding that decision to leave him on the bench, was subsequently given a 30-day suspension by the FPF on October 19.

With the Selecao facing Denmark and Wales in the Nations League on November 14 and 17, that would appear to mean there will be no farewell appearance for the five-time Ballon d'Or winner, who posted an emotional video statement on Instagram on Thursday.

Ronaldo said: "The time has come for me to hand back the number seven shirt of our national team.

A post shared by Cristiano Ronaldo (@cristiano)

"I never felt it belonged to me alone, but for 23 years, I had the privilege and the responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me into stadiums all around the world. Today, the time has come for me to pass it on to the future.

"It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the mind alone, they are also made with the heart. A journey that started in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end.

"I made mistakes, I suffered, there were times where I could not give the Portuguese people what they so desperately wanted. But I never gave up, and above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

"If I ever made you happy, if one of my goals made you embrace someone or feel proud of our country, then it was all worth it.

"I leave with my head held high, proud of the path I have taken and knowing I gave everything I had for Portugal. I will never stop being one of you.

"I spent my life working to become the best player in the world. Today, I learn that my greatest achievement was earning the honour of representing the best country in the world."

10 – Cristiano Ronaldo is the only to have scored at least 10 goals at both the FIFA World Cup (11) and UEFA European Championship finals (14). Perfect. https://t.co/HPOUYCOfEv

Ronaldo retires as the only player to have scored at least 10 goals at both the World Cup (11) and the European Championships (14), with the latter figure being a competition record.

He became the first player to score at six different editions of the World Cup this year, when he helped himself to a brace in a group-stage win over Uzbekistan, while he is also the oldest player to feature in a World Cup knockout-stage match, at the age of 41.

However, none of Ronaldo's World Cup goals came in the knockout stages, and he was also memorably dropped by Fernando Santos during the 2022 edition, which was won by his great rival Lionel Messi and Argentina.

Ronaldo's international retirement comes less than a month after Messi made his final appearance for the Albiceleste, bowing out during a 3-0 win over Benin.

Will Cristiano Ronaldo get a Portugal farewell match? What we know after retirement announcement

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo has announced his retirement from the Portuguese national side following 23 years with the team.

Ronaldo’s international future already looked doubtful following a very public spat with coach Jorge Jesus but one very small fine later and it appeared he was back in contention.

However, he has now taken to X to confirm he is stepping away from the Portuguese national team and in his own words “Handing back the number seven shirt.”

“To the Portuguese people, the day has come for me to hand back the number seven shirt of our national team.

“I never it belonged to me alone. But for 23 years I had the privilege and responsibility of wearing it, defending it and carrying it with me into stadiums.

“Today, the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision because some decisions are not made with the mind alone. they are also made with the heart.”

There is the question of whether Ronaldo will play one final match for the team, having last featured against Norway in September. Neither player nor team has confirmed whether he is finished now or in the near future with Portugal’s next game being a Nations League tie against Denmark on November 14.

In a statement, the Portuguese national team hinted at a final match by promising a “due tribute.”

“The Portuguese Football Federation expresses its deepest and most enduring gratitude to its eternal captain, Cristiano Ronaldo, who today announced his definitive retirement from the National Team.

“It is with a feeling of profound gratitude and immense admiration that the Portuguese Football Federation publicly praises and thanks Cristiano Ronaldo for his absolutely extraordinary career serving our country.

Comparing Lionel Messi and Cristiano Ronaldo’s final international stats

“More than just an outstanding athlete, our captain was an inspiring leader, an example of dedication, and the ultimate symbol of the spirit of our National Team.

“Throughout a legendary international career, marked by 234 caps and 146 goals scored, the captain carried the country on his shoulders, broke records, and led the team to moments that will forever be etched in the history of national football.

“Every drop of sweat, every decisive goal, and every captain’s armband worn with pride on the field reflected his unconditional love for our jersey.

“His dedication and legacy transcend the four lines of the pitch.

“Cristiano Ronaldo has elevated Portugal’s name and inspired generations of young people who dream of following in his footsteps, being, for many of us, the best player of all time!

“Therefore, the Portuguese Football Federation refuses to let this farewell take place without due tribute.

“It is our firm and immense desire to organise, plan, participate and actively collaborate in what will always be a fitting tribute to Cristiano Ronaldo’s immense career, in which fans and the country can applaud and celebrate, once again, the player who has given us so much.”

“Forceful and Aggressive” Parker’s Passion Already Making A Mark At West Ham

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham manager Scott Parker’s comments following last night’s incredible comeback against Cardiff City were an absolute joy to listen to. Having watched the Hammers recover from three goals down to draw 3-3, I was already feeling considerably more optimistic about our prospects. But listening to Parker afterwards was almost as encouraging as the second-half performance itself.

In the modern game, much is made of tactics, and rightly so. But one thing I believe gets underplayed is not only man-management, but a head coach’s personality. And quite frankly, Parker has bundles of it.

I have to say, it’s a refreshing change from what we’ve seen in recent seasons. Nuno Espírito Santo was a softly spoken man, not prone to using a few words when considerably more would do, and the less said about Graham Potter as a leader and orator, the better at this point. But my word, it’s thrilling to hear Parker speak.

Speaking in his post-match interview with West Ham’s official website, Parker described the transformation in his team after the break:

“It was a forceful team, an aggressive team, a front-footed team, which was obviously a big improvement.”

That’s exactly what I want to hear from a West Ham manager, and more importantly, it’s exactly what I want to see from a West Ham team.

As Martins morning round-up demonstrated, the difference between the two halves was extraordinary.

We went from a team seemingly incapable of stringing together a meaningful attack to one that looked determined to take the game to the opposition.

And Parker clearly recognised the difference.

He was particularly critical of the backwards passing and the lack of purpose in possession during the opening 45 minutes.

Then came another observation that I found particularly encouraging:

“There’s no point in just building, building, building.”

Again, music to my ears and we saw that with the first two goals from Mukasa and Engels.

For far too long, we’ve watched West Ham move the ball around without appearing particularly interested in doing anything with it.

Parker wants players taking risks, making forward runs, delivering crosses and getting bodies into the penalty area.

That doesn’t guarantee success, of course, but it’s a pretty good place to start.

What really impressed me, though, wasn’t simply Parker’s analysis of the football.

It cannot be overstated quite how much good it does the fanbase to see a manager walking over to applaud the travelling supporters, becoming one with them, and then speaking so passionately about what he wants to achieve.

There were 3,200 West Ham fans at Cardiff, and after the dreadful opening 45 minutes, they deserved something to cheer.

And I’ll tell you this much: if Parker communicates with supporters like this, one can only imagine how effective he might be when speaking to his players.

Of course, being a good speaker doesn’t automatically make somebody a good manager.

But I’ve always believed that one of the most immediate improvements West Ham needed was motivation.

We have talented players. I’ve said that repeatedly. What we’ve lacked is somebody capable of getting them playing with urgency, belief and a bit of fire in their bellies.

Parker certainly sounds like somebody who understands that.

I was also absolutely delighted to hear him say how eager he was to get the players together and start asking questions.

He admitted that he couldn’t fully understand how the performance had changed so dramatically between the two halves.

And rather than simply celebrating the comeback, he was already thinking about what needed to improve before Saturday’s trip to Blackburn Rovers.

It’s also worth remembering that Parker had only met the players at the airport that morning, having been confirmed as West Ham’s new head coach just hours before kick-off.

He has barely had an opportunity to work with them.

Judging his tactics, selections and long-term suitability can come later. There will be plenty of time for that.

But I’ve always thought there was one obvious and immediate improvement that could be made to this West Ham team, and that was getting the players motivated.

After listening to Parker, I have considerably fewer worries on that score.

I genuinely cannot wait for the next game.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

“I can tell you this story. I was at Milan, we were playing against Roma, and the coach who would replace me had already been hired [Conceiçao]. The next day I had to quickly clear out my room and locker because the new coach wanted to come in.

“And you know what? I didn’t even get a phone call, but there was no need. In football, these situations are part of the game, and I didn’t take it personally. We must understand, without hypocrisy, that these processes are natural.”

Cristiano Ronaldo retires from international football

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Ronaldo calls time on his career with Portugal as international football’s all-time top scorer after a dispute with his country’s federation

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international football.

Ronaldo, 41, calls time on his career with Portugal after a legendary 23-year stint, scoring 146 goals in 234 games to become international football’s all-time top scorer and appearance maker.

He captained his nation to their first ever major title at Euro 2016, while also lifting two Nations League titles in 2019 and 2025.

“The day has come back for me to hand back the number 7 shirt of our national team,” he said in an announcement on social video. “I never felt it belonged to me alone. But for 23 years I had the privilege and the responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me into stadiums all around the world.

“Today, the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision. Because some decisions are not made with the mind alone. They are also made with the heart.

“A journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end. When I look back at everything with the national team, I don’t just see the matches, goals, records or trophies.

“I see people. I see the Portuguese people living abroad who, for 90 minutes, felt a little closer to home. I see millions of Portuguese people I have never met but who became part of my life. Because at the end of the day, football is so much bigger than a game. Football is about people.”

The five-time Ballon d’Or winner’s retirement follows his controversial decision to quit the Portugal camp during the international break last month, where he accused new manager Jorge Jesus of twice breaking his word after remaining an unused substitute against Norway.

The Portuguese FA handed Ronaldo a 30-day suspension - which would be served before the November international break - as well as imposing a €255 (£214.88) fine, with Jesus having not closed the door on bringing the country’s captain back into the fold.

However, Ronaldo has now taken the decision to permanently step away from Portugal duty, a month after storied rival Lionel Messi played his last game for Argentina.

Ronaldo made his Portugal debut in August 2003 when he came on as a half-time substitute for fellow legend Rui Costa in a 1-0 friendly win over Kazakhstan, eight days after completing his move from Sporting CP to Manchester United.

He scored his first goal at Euro 2004, bagging in a 2-1 group-stage loss to Greece, who Portugal would eventually lose to again in the final. He was named in the team of the tournament at 19.

Ronaldo was involved in an infamous incident with club team-mate Wayne Rooney in the Euro 2006 quarter-finals, with Ronaldo rushing over to the referee to protest after the Englishman was involved in a tangle with Ricardo Carvalho. Rooney was subsequently shown red, with Ronaldo caught winking on camera to add further fuel to the fire.

Ronaldo went on to appear at a joint-most six World Cup finals and an outright-most six European Championships, with his crowning glory coming in France in 2016.

Ronaldo’s best World Cup finish saw Portugal beaten in the semi-finals by France in 2006, with Zinedine Zidane’s penalty proving the difference in a 1-0 win.

His final attempt to claim a World Cup winners’ medal ended in tearful failure this year when Portugal were knocked out by eventual champions Spain in the last-16, courtesy of Mikel Merino’s stoppage-time winner.

“Throughout my entire career with the national team I always pushed myself beyond my limits,” he continued in his statement.

“I wanted to win, I wanted to be better, I wanted to make history. I always demanded the most of myself. I always wanted more. I always believed we could go further. And I gave everything I had for this shirt.

“There were perfect afternoons and nights. There were victories I will never forget. There were defeats that still hurt to remember.

“I made mistakes. I suffered. There were also moments when I couldn’t give the Portuguese people what they so desperately wanted. But I never gave up. And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal”

He remains 15 strikes short of 1,000-goal target before he retires from football for good, with his only scoring avenue coming in Saudi Arabia with current club Al-Nassr.

Cristiano Ronaldo's statement in full as he retires from international football

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Ronaldo announced his retirement from Portugal duty after scoring 146 goals in 234 games, although his international career ended in acrimony after walking out on the team last month

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international football at the age of 41, hanging up his boots after a legendary career with Portugal.

Ronaldo played for Portugal for 23 years, scoring a ludicrous 146 goals in 234 games to become international football’s all-time top scorer, and captained his country to their first major title when they triumphed at Euro 2016.

He also lifted Nations League crowns in 2019 and 2025, although his time on the international stage ended in acrimony after he walked out of Portugal camp last month after being left as an unused substitute in a match against Norway – claiming new manager Jorge Jesus twice broke promises to him.

The Portuguese FA handed Ronaldo a 30-day suspension, which ran out before the November international break, and fined him a paltry €255 (£214.88) but he has now stepped away from the national side more than 23 years after making his debut against Kazakhstan in August 2003.

He announced his decision in a video on Instagram and will now continue to strive for his stated target of 1,000 career goals at club side Al-Nassr in Saudi Arabia, needing just 15 more to reach the remarkable milestone. Here’s everything Ronaldo had to say about his retirement:

“The day has come back for me to hand back the number 7 shirt of our national team. I never felt it belonged to me alone. But for 23 years I had the privilege and the responsibility of wearing it, defending it, and carrying it with me into stadiums all around the world.

“Today, the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision. Because some decisions are not made with the mind alone. They are also made with the heart.

“A journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end. When I look back at everything with the national team, I don’t just see the matches, goals, records or trophies.

“I see people. I see the Portuguese people living abroad who, for 90 minutes, felt a little closer to home. I see millions of Portuguese people I have never met but who became part of my life. Because at the end of the day, football is so much bigger than a game. Football is about people.

“Throughout my entire career with the national team I always pushed myself beyond my limits. I wanted to win, I wanted to be better, I wanted to make history. I always demanded the most of myself. I always wanted more. I always believed we could go further. And I gave everything I had for this shirt.

“There were perfect afternoons and nights. There were victories I will never forget. There were defeats that still hurt to remember.

“I made mistakes. I suffered. There were also moments when I couldn’t give the Portuguese people what they so desperately wanted. But I never gave up. And above all, I never stopped feeling proud to represent Portugal.

“Over the years, I learned a few very simple things: Goals fade. Trophies end up in museums. Records are eventually broken. But there is something that time can never erase: the place we hold in people’s hearts. If I ever made you happy, if one of my goals ever made you embrace someone, forget a worry, or feel proud of your country, then it was all worth it. Because being Portuguese is also about this. We suffer together. We rise together. We believe again.

“Portugal will always need young people who dare to dream. Players who never give up. New generations who believe it is possible to go where no one has gone before. Because a country that keeps creating new heroes is a country that never stops believing in itself.”

Ronaldo's most iconic moments as he retires from international football

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo became the first player to win five Champions League trophies and remains the top scorer in the tournament's history

Cristiano Ronaldo says his international career with Portugal is over, meaning the 41-year-old will just play on with Al-Nassr.

The main thing driving him on now is hoping to reach an unprecedented 1,000 career goals - he is currently on 985.

BBC Sport looks at some of the most iconic moments of a career that has also taken in spells at Sporting, Manchester United, Real Madrid and Juventus.

Ronaldo spurred on by 1,000 goals and Portugal glory

Ronaldo will not retire until he scores 1,000 goals

Untouchable or a support act? Ronaldo's 1,000-goal quest continues

Cristiano Ronaldo had one season in the Sporting first team before moving to Old Trafford

After 31 first-team games and five goals for Sporting, Ronaldo caught the eye of Manchester United in a pre-season friendly in 2003, setting up two goals in a 3-1 win in Lisbon.

Ronaldo revealed that United boss Alex Ferguson took him into the dressing room afterwards to meet the players.

Five days later he joined the Red Devils for Â£12m.

"After we played Sporting the lads in the dressing room talked about him constantly, and on the plane back from the game they urged me to sign him - that's how highly they rated him," said Ferguson.

"He is one of the most exciting young players I've ever seen."

Cristiano Ronaldo has won league titles in England, Spain, Italy and Saudi Arabia

Ronaldo won the FA Cup in his first season at United, scoring in the final against Millwall.

But it was in 2006-07 that his career really exploded. He hit 17 Premier League goals, his first season of double figures, was named player of the year and young player of the year - and won his first league title.

The following season was even better, winning his first Champions League - scoring in the final - his first European Golden Shoe for top scorer, and another league title.

Cristiano Ronaldo won the first Puskas award - and it remains the only Champions League goal to win it

Ronaldo scored 118 goals in 292 games in his first spell at United, and one was ranked above all the others.

He hit an absolute rocket of a shot into the top corner from 40 yards in his homeland against Porto, a goal which took them into the Champions League semi-finals.

It won the inaugural Fifa Puskas award for goal of the year.

Months later, Ronaldo joined Real Madrid for Â£80m.

Cristiano Ronaldo helped Real Madrid to win their first Copa del Rey in seven years

Ronaldo's first season at Real Madrid produced no trophies and his second saw just the Copa del Rey won.

That final was especially notable because it is the first time Ronaldo had scored the winning goal in a final. And it was in El Clasico, one of four meetings in 18 days in all competitions.

He rose at the far post to power in a trademark header from Angel di Maria's cross in extra time as Real beat Barcelona 1-0.

Cristiano Ronaldo scored 14 goals in 14 games against Levante

Ronaldo, then 30, scored in a 3-0 La Liga win over Levante in October 2015 to become the club's all-time top scorer with his 324th goal in 310 games.

The 2015-16 campaign would go on to be the sixth season in a row that Ronaldo scored 50 or more goals in all competitions.

Ronaldo ended up extending that record by 126 goals - leaving Real with 450 goals in just 438 games.

Cristiano Ronaldo is the top scorer in European Championship history - with 14 goals - as well as lifting the trophy once

The one thing missing from Ronaldo's CV was international success.

And that came in 2016 when Portugal won the European Championship by beating hosts France 1-0 in the final in Paris.

Ronaldo ended up taking on an unofficial coaching role in that final after going off injured in tears midway through the first half.

He was named in the team of the tournament after three goals and three assists. Ronaldo retires as the top scorer in Euros history, with 14 goals.

Cristiano Ronaldo has won five Ballons d'Or, only behind Lionel Messi

Cristiano Ronaldo won his fifth Ballon d'Or, equalling Lionel Messi's record at the time, after winning the Champions League and La Liga in 2016-17. He netted twice in that European final over Juventus.

It followed on from the best player in the world awards in 2008, 2013, 2014 and 2016.

Sadly for Ronaldo, Messi would go on to win three more Ballons d'Or, meaning he won their personal rivalry 8-5.

Cristiano Ronaldo scored one of his eight Champions League hat-tricks in the 2017 win over Bayern Munich

Ronaldo became the first player to score 100 Champions League goals with a hat-trick in the 2016-17 quarter-final second leg win over Bayern Munich.

Only Messi and Robert Lewandowski have joined him to date in the century club.

Ronaldo would go on to score 141 goals in 187 games (also a record) in Europe's top competition. For six seasons in a row he was the tournament's top scorer or joint top scorer.

"That is one of the great goals you will seeâ¦ I was going to say Champions League football, but, basically, football," said Pat Nevin in BBC Radio 5 Live commentary

In 2018 came one of the most iconic moments of Ronaldo's career, maybe the greatest Champions League goal ever scored.

In the quarter-final, Ronaldo leapt into the air before acrobatically firing into the corner of the net with an incredible bicycle kick against Juventus.

Even the Juve fans stood up to applaud the player who would join their club months later.

It was nominated for the Puskas Award but he finished second to Mohamed Salah's goal for Liverpool against Everton.

The night Juventus rose to applaud Ronaldo

Cristiano Ronaldo was the first man to win five Champions Leagues, although he was not the last as Real Madrid kept winning it without him

Ronaldo won two La Liga titles and two Copa del Rey trophies at Real Madrid, but it was in Europe's elite club competition where he became a true legend.

He had already won the Champions League in 2008 with United, and went on to win it four more times with Real.

The first was in 2014 before a remarkable three times in a row in 2016, 2017 and 2018 (beating Liverpool in the Gareth Bale/Loris Karius final) to become the first player to win the Champions League five times.

Since his departure, former Real team-mates Toni Kroos, Dani Carvajal, Luka Modric and Nacho have gone on to win six.

Cristiano Ronaldo had a penalty saved by Gavin Bazunu before netting twice to deny Ireland a famous win in Faro

Ali Daei scored 109 goals for Iran between 1993 and 2006 - and it was felt like maybe his world record for international goals in men's football would never be broken.

But Ronaldo's two late headers in a World Cup qualifying win over the Republic of Ireland in 2021 took him onto 111 goals.

He kept adding to that total and finished on 146 goals in 234 caps - also a record for international appearances.

Cristiano Ronaldo was the first player in professional football to ever hit 900 official goals

Ronaldo continued to smash the goals in for Juventus, in his second spell at United and at Al-Nassr but failed to really replicate many truly magic moments of the past.

However he did create some history in 2024 as he became the first player to score 900 top-level career goals as Portugal beat Croatia.

That number is now 985 - so he is only 15 off an unprecedented 1,000th goal.

He has now hit five for Sporting, 450 for Real Madrid, 145 for Manchester United, 101 for Juventus, 138 for Al-Nassr and 146 for Portugal.

He recently confirmed that he is aiming to hit the 1,000 mark.

But old rival Messi is on 931 goals, the second highest scorer ever, so Ronaldo cannot afford to dawdle too much...

Untouchable or a support act? Ronaldo's 1,000-goal quest continues

Brighton's XI at Getafe predicted: A huge Pascal Gross decision for Fabian Hurzeler

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Brighton have a number of selection headscratchers ahead of their Uefa Conference League clash at Getafe.

The Seagulls are in Madrid looking for their first away win in the tournament but also have a tough Premier League fixture against Brentford at the Amex Stadium on Sunday.

Did you know with an ad-lite subscription to SussexWorld, you get 70% fewer ads while viewing the news that matters to you.

Head coach Fabian Hurzeler will carefully manage the energy levels of his squad, including a big call on veteran midfielder Pascal Gross. The 35-year-old has started every match this season and also took a whack to the foot at the end of Saturday’s 1-0 win at Manchester City.

"We expect a tough opponent," said Hurzeler. "Because I think when you play here in Getafe and when you see them playing also, for example, at the weekend against Sevilla, you know it's a team that knows how to win.

"It's a team that plays quite vertical, long balls, second balls, [who are] good in personal duels, and can be very intense in personal duels.

"Therefore, we expect a team that has a clear style of play combined with individual quality."

Here's how the Seagulls could line up against Getafe at the Estadio Coliseum

Bart Verbruggen - GK: The Dutchman was superb in the 1-0 win at Manchester City and will look to keep another clean sheet in Madrid. He dismissed links of a potential move to La Liga ahead of the Getafe match and said he’s happy at Brighton. Hooray!

Costinha - RB: A tough choice here as ideally he needs a rest after back to back 90 minutes against City and a potential match against Brentford on Sunday. Mats Wieffer is ineligible for the Conference League stage and Kadioglu is sidelined with a hip issue.

Michael Svoboda - CB: The Austrian has impressed during his time on the pitch and expect him to get another start at centre back tonight. Lewis Dunk and Luka Vuskovic - both superb at City - could be rested with Brentford in mind.

Olivier Boscagli - CB: The Frenchman could be back in his preferred position of left sided centre back. Seems to struggle a little at left back.

Jaouen Hadjam - LB: The French-born Algerian has made a positive impact to the squad since arriving from Swiss club Young Boys. Another very shrewd signing.

Pascal Gross - CM: Of course he plays, it’s Pascal Gross. He probably does need a rest though and the 35-year-old took a whack to the foot at the end of the City match. Interesting decision here and Malick Yalcouye could also be an option.

Chema Andres - CM: Yasin Ayari (or Gross) may get a rest here but I expect Chema to start in midfield in his home City of Madrid. He’s made a brilliant start to his Albion career.

Diego Gomez - RW: Zadok Yohanna is getting fitter but I expect Gomez to start this one with Yankuba Minteh (ineligible for Conference League) a likely starter on the right against Brentford on Sunday.

Jack Hinshelwood - No 10: The academy graduate will likely be a starter in the No 10 as he gets back to his best after injury.

Ibrahim Osman - LW: The flying winger could get another chance to impress on the left flank but once again, Yalcouye is an option here too. Kaoru Mitoma remains injured.

Georginio - CF: The Frenchman impressed from the bench at City and should lead the line ahead of Premier League regular Charalampos Kostoulas.

Ronaldo retires from Portugal duty

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo won Euro 2016 with Portugal

Portugal legend Cristiano Ronaldo says he is retiring from international football.

The 41-year-old forward has scored 146 goals and won 234 caps for his country - both world records - since making his debut in 2003.

He walked out of the Portugal camp in September after being told he would not start the Nations League match against Denmark.

"To the Portuguese people, the day has come for me to hand back the number seven shirt of our national team," Ronaldo said in an Instagram video., external

"I never felt it belonged to me alone, but for 23 years I had the privilege and responsibility of wearing it, defending it and carrying it with me into stadiums all around the world.

"Today the time has come to pass it on to the future. It was not an easy decision, because some decisions are not made with the mind alone - they are also made with the heart.

"A journey that began in August 2003 and changed my life forever has come to an end."

CR7's legacy - watch all of Ronaldo's 11 World Cup goals

On Friday Portugal are set to announce their squad for November's Nations League fixtures - against Denmark at home and Wales away.

The Portugal Football Federation said it refuses to "let this farewell take place without due tribute".

A statement read: "It is our firm and immense desire to organise, plan, participate and actively collaborate in what will always be a fitting tribute to Cristiano Ronaldo's immense career, in which fans and the country can applaud and celebrate, once again, the player who has given us so much."

Ronaldo's great rival and Argentina legend Lionel Messi retired from international football in October.

Fantasy football Start ‘Em, Sit ‘Em: 30 players to start or sit in Week 9

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season has already brought with it plenty of chaos, as several teams are making notable moves at the quarterback position.

A microcosm of the league's greater movement can be seen with the Baltimore Ravens. The Ravens lost Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley to long-term injuries and will start undrafted rookie Joe Fagnano on "Thursday Night Football." However, they are also planning to trade for Arizona Cardinals quarterback Jacoby Brissett over the weekend, so their quarterback room is in flux.

The same is true with the Seattle Seahawks, where Drew Lock will replace an injured Sam Darnold, and with the Cleveland Browns, where Shedeur Sanders may start with Deshaun Watson in concussion protocol. And that's not to mention the Philadelphia Eagles' decision to bench Tanner McKee to see what they have in the potential-packed Joe Milton.

That swath of quarterback changes will leave many fantasy managers with difficult start 'em, sit 'em decisions. Those quandaries will also exist at other positions like running back, where fantasy footballers will have to decide whether to trust Ashton Jeanty or Tyler Allgeier, among others.

Who can you start and sit in fantasy football for Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season? USA TODAY Sports outlines 30 players to start or sit.

Malik Willis, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

Willis is starting to round into form for the Dolphins and has scored multiple touchdowns in three consecutive games. During that span, he has averaged 37.7 rushing yards per game, which will raise his floor in most matchups. The Lions have allowed the most fantasy points per game (FPPG) to quarterbacks this season, so Willis should be positioned to have a strong showing in what figures to be a favorable, high-scoring matchup.

Drew Lock, Seattle Seahawks (vs. Arizona Cardinals)

The Cardinals are one of just three NFL teams to allow 18 passing touchdowns to quarterbacks this season, along with the Lions and the Las Vegas Raiders. Unsurprisingly, Arizona has allowed a league-high 29.8 fantasy points per game to the position because of that, so Lock should be positioned for a nice outing in which he could toss multiple touchdowns.

Joe Milton, Philadelphia Eagles (vs. New York Giants)

If you're looking for a fun dart throw, Milton could be worth considering. He's set to start in place of Tanner McKee and has the cannon arm and elite athleticism needed to blossom into a fantasy asset. The Giants have allowed the seventh-most FPPG to quarterbacks, so Milton has a chance to emerge as a decent streamer.

Quinshon Judkins, Cleveland Browns (at New Orleans Saints)

Judkins has been boom-or-bust as a touchdown-dependent fantasy option, but he should be poised for a good outing against the Saints. New Orleans has allowed the most FPPG to running backs this season, including a league-high 11 rushing touchdowns to the position. With that in mind, Judkins should be able to find his way into the end zone.

Jonah Coleman, Denver Broncos (at Carolina Panthers)

Coleman took on a lion's share of Denver's backfield touches in Week 9, posting 104 total yards and a touchdown on 15 touches (11 carries, four receptions). The rookie should continue in that role and has a great matchup against a Panthers defense that has allowed the second-most FPPG to running backs this season.

Tyler Allgeier, Arizona Cardinals (at Seattle Seahawks)

Seattle is a tough matchup for any running back, but Allgeier figures to get great volume with Jeremiyah Love (ankle) on IR and Bam Knight dealing with a concussion. That could be enough to make him a touchdown-dependent flex play after he popped off for 191 total yards and two touchdowns against the Cowboys.

Jadarian Price, Seattle Seahawks (vs. Arizona Cardinals)

Price and Zach Charbonnet split carries for the Seahawks against the Bears. Neither was particularly effective but each gets a much easier matchup in Week 9 against a Cardinals defense that has been middle-of-the-pack against running backs this season. Price has some explosiveness, so don't be surprised if this is his breakout game.

Caleb Douglas, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

As mentioned, the Lions have been weak against the pass this year. If we're recommending to start Willis, then Douglas should get a nod too, as the rookie has notched six receptions for 113 yards and two touchdowns in two games since returning from an early-season ankle injury.

Ladd McConkey, Los Angeles Chargers (vs. Houston Texans)

Trusting McConkey may seem risky, given that Justin Herbert has struggled under pressure this season. That said, the Texans are allowing the second-most FPPG to receivers this season and McConkey is fresh off a four-catch, 86-yard outing that was one of his best of the season. He should be in line for a solid showing against the Texans.

Josh Downs, Indianapolis Colts (vs. Dallas Cowboys)

Could this be a showcase game for Downs ahead of the 2026 NFL trade deadline? It sure feels like it. Dallas has allowed the fifth-most FPPG to receivers this season, and the Colts may be eager to boost Downs' trade value by targeting him early and often in this spot.

Brian Thomas Jr., Jacksonville Jaguars (at Baltimore Ravens)

The same is true of Thomas, who could be traded as he sees his role shrink in Jacksonville. He caught a touchdown last week and could be in line for ample playing time if the Jaguars can build a big lead against a Joe Fagnano-led Ravens team on "Thursday Night Football."

Mike Gesicki, Cincinnati Bengals (at Atlanta Falcons)

Gesicki has scored in five consecutive games and six of the seven he has played this season. Joe Burrow has thrown 13 touchdown passes over his last three games, so Gesicki should continue to have high touchdown upside regardless of his opponent.

Greg Dulcich, Miami Dolphins (vs. Detroit Lions)

Dulcich has posted at least four catches and 37 yards in four of his last five games. Now, he gets to face a Lions defense that is allowing the most receptions (61), receiving yards (684) and receiving touchdowns (11) to tight ends this season. He could emerge as a surprise top-10 tight end this week.

Jacksonville Jaguars (at Baltimore Ravens)

The Jaguars are playing the Ravens on a short week. Baltimore is expected to start third-string quarterback Joe Fagnano, an undrafted rookie, after injuries to Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley. Fagnano completed 10 of 21 passes for 91 yards and an interception while being sacked three times against the Bills. The Jaguars should pressure him into numerous mistakes while their top-ranked run defense should be able to bottle up Derrick Henry.

The Lions will likely give up some yardage and points to the Dolphins, but they should also generate plenty of sacks. Detroit has 24 sacks this season, third-most in the NFL, while Malik Willis has been sacked 21 times through seven starts.

Bryce Young, Carolina Panthers (vs. Denver Broncos)

Young has regressed over the last two weeks after a torrid start to the season, completing just 54.4% of his passes for 372 yards, two touchdowns and three interceptions during Carolina's two-game losing skid. The main issue? Young has been pressured a lot in those contests, and he has just a 64 passer rating when pressured compared to 106.3 when kept clean, per Pro Football Focus. The Broncos rank fourth in pressure rate league-wide, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats, so they should be able to limit Young in this favorable spot.

Bo Nix, Denver Broncos (at Carolina Panthers)

The Panthers have a banged-up secondary, but it still won't be easy to justify starting Nix. The Broncos quarterback has looked better recently, throwing for exactly 222 yards in back-to-back games and scoring twice in each. Still, the Panthers are typically more susceptible against the run, so Nix has a lower ceiling than most streaming options this week.

Daniel Jones, Indianapolis Colts (vs. Dallas Cowboys)

Normally, the quarterback playing the Cowboys is a solid streaming option. However, with Jones facing down the potential for a benching and having failed to throw multiple touchdown passes in a game this season, it's best to steer clear of him.

Ashton Jeanty, Las Vegas Raiders (at San Francisco 49ers)

Can you really trust Jeanty after the Raiders gave him just two carries against the Jets while giving Mike Washington Jr. a whopping 25? Jeanty will remain productive as a receiver, but his uncertain between-the-tackles workload makes it hard to trust the former first-round pick, especially against a 49ers defense allowing just 3.6 yards per carry to running backs this season. It's better to wait and see whether Jeanty's decrease in carries was punishment for trash-talking the Jets before Week 8 or a sign of what's to come in Klint Kubiak's running back rotation.

Breece Hall, New York Jets (at Kansas City Chiefs)

Hall seems likely to return from a nagging quad injury this week, but he gets a tough matchup against a Chiefs defense that has largely bottled up opposing running backs this season. Hall may also be splitting carries with Braelon Allen, who has thrived in his stead, so this doesn't seem like an ideal spot to trust him.

Aaron Jones and Jordan Mason, Minnesota Vikings (at Buffalo Bills)

We'll do two for the price of one here. The Vikings have a bottom-six rushing offense this season in terms of EPA per play, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats. Jones and Mason are also stuck in a frustrating time share, so neither seems like a particularly viable play against a Bills defense that has allowed just 97.4 rushing yards per game this season.

Michael Wilson, Arizona Cardinals (at Seattle Seahawks)

Wilson finally had a breakout game for the first time since Marvin Harrison Jr. was traded, generating eight catches for 111 yards and a touchdown against the Cowboys. He'll have a much tougher matchup against a Seahawks team that has been excellent against receivers this year, especially after Wilson suffered a couple of broken ribs on his touchdown catch.

Adonai Mitchell, New York Jets (at Kansas City Chiefs)

Mitchell just logged seven catches for 142 yards and a touchdown against the Raiders in Week 8. He gets a much tougher matchup in Week 9 against a Chiefs defense that has allowed the second-fewest FPPG to receivers this season. Add in that Omar Cooper Jr. could also siphon targets from him and Mitchell may be a hit-or-miss play each week even if Garrett Wilson (concussion) is unable to play.

Jalen Coker, Carolina Panthers (vs. Denver Broncos)

The Broncos get a lot of pressure, so Young won't have much time to throw in that matchup. That could limit Coker's deep-ball upside, and while he could rack up yards after the catch, it's hard to trust him to do so against a quality defense.

Khalil Shakir, Buffalo Bills (at Minnesota Vikings)

Shakir started well in the Bills' win over the Ravens but did little outside the first drive. DJ Moore seems likely to continue getting most of the attention from Josh Allen, so don't be surprised if Shakir struggles against a Vikings defense that has allowed the third-fewest FPPG to receivers this season.

Dalton Kincaid, Buffalo Bills (at Minnesota Vikings)

Kincaid scored a touchdown last week for the first time since Week 2, but he will be hard-pressed to repeat that feat against the Vikings. Minnesota has yet to allow a receiving touchdown to tight ends this season, so Kincaid figures to be limited.

T.J. Hockenson, Minnesota Vikings (vs. Buffalo Bills)

The Bills have only allowed one touchdown to tight ends this season. It also isn't clear whether Kyler Murray (hamstring) will play in this contest, so Hockenson might have a lower ceiling in this matchup than most.

No NFL team has allowed fewer points per game than the Vikings (13.3). No NFL team has scored more points per game than the Bills (33.3). Something will give in this matchup, but if there's one quarterback that's built to beat Brian Flores' pressure-happy scheme, it's Josh Allen.

San Francisco 49ers (vs. Las Vegas Raiders)

The 49ers are favored to beat the Raiders, but this could end up being a shootout given how Kirk Cousins and Co. have been playing. Las Vegas is averaging 27.6 points per game, so even if Nick Bosa returns, this is still a low-floor spot for San Francisco.

Klopp explains surprise decision to call up Gladbach prodigy Wael Mohya to Germany squad

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Germany head coach Jürgen Klopp pulled a surprise out of the hat on Thursday by naming 17-year-old Borussia Mönchengladbach prodigy Wael Mohya in his squad for the first time following the youngster's impressive start to the season.

In an interview published by the German Football Association (DFB), Klopp explained his decision to include the highly-rated attacking midfielder.

“He's one of those cases where you simply have to take notice. There aren't many 17-year-olds currently playing in the Bundesliga. That alone is an outstanding achievement. But he's also doing well – really well. I've been in contact with both him and his family, and we've discussed how we want to approach things. I'm really keen to build the future together with these boys, including Wael, without making any promises or anything like that,” he said, adding:

“I would say that I have been working with young, highly talented people for quite some time now, and it is something I am very passionate about. I explained this to the Mohya family, and we then agreed that we would bring him along to give him a chance to experience it and see how things go.”

Klopp went on to stress that there are no guarantees Mohya will make the squad for either of the upcoming games against Serbia and the Netherlands, explaining that the main priority is to see the 17-year-old in training and how he conducts himself as part of the national team setup.

“Above all, the important thing is to let him experience the training sessions firsthand, because I think that is really valuable when you have the opportunity. And we would like to continue doing this in the future whenever possible: always having a place in the squad where we can say, ‘Okay, come along.’ Let's see how he does, whether he plays, and whether he even makes it into any of the matchday squads. Of course, it will always depend on his performances in training. That's only logical,” Klopp said.

"But it's also about seeing how a young guy conducts himself, both on and off the pitch, and having conversations with him. I've had three conversations with him so far, and they've all been excellent, I have to say. I wasn't as self-confident as he is at 17, but then again, I wasn't as good as he is at 17 either! So I'm really looking forward to having Wael with us."

Mohya, who is also eligible to represent Morocco, only made his debut for Germany's U21 side in September.

He has three goals and one assist in 10 games for Gladbach this season.
