Enzo Maresca speaks out on Man City's reliance on Erling Haaland after Premier League setbacks

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Manchester City have lost their last two Premier League games to fall behind Arsenal in the table

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Enzo Maresca has warned his Manchester City squad that they cannot simply rely on Erling Haaland for goals after losing their last two Premier League games.

The striker failed to find the net on Saturday as City lost 1-0 at Brighton, and that defeat cost the team their place at the top of the Premier League as Arsenal took full advantage by beating Liverpool the following day.

City were also beaten 2-1 by Aston Villa prior to the Brighton loss, and Maresca believes it is essential that other players begin adding to the goals tally to help Haaland.

The City manager said: “It’s very important that in each game, we created chances. For sure, when you have Erling inside the pitch, it’s better because he’s going to score goals, or you know he’s always there close.

“At the same time, we cannot rely on Erling for every game. We need also the rest to score goals.

“But, again, I think the last game against Brighton was the only game of the season where we didn’t score. But, for sure, if Erling is on the pitch we are happy.”

Maresca was speaking to media in Germany ahead of City’s Champions League league phase match against RB Leipzig on Wednesday.

City have made a solid start in the competition, with victories over Porto and AEK Athens. The one setback came last month, when they were beaten by holders PSG.

Phil Foden stood out against the Greek side, scoring a hat-trick in an encouraging sign that the 2024 player of the year is returning to his best.

The midfielder was dropped by England for last summer’s World Cup after two indifferent seasons with City and also served a domestic suspension earlier in the season after being sent off in the Manchester derby.

Foden said: “Obviously I had the unfortunate red card against Man United but I think the break did me good. I came back really fresh and sharp.

“I’ve been involved in a lot of goals lately and I’ve been really happy with my own performance. Hopefully I can get back to that level. I think I’m starting to get back there now. Hopefully I keep growing and getting better.”

Asked about his England prospects, he said: “It is one of those things. Football is full of ups and downs and it’s one of those things you can’t control.

“You just have to keep trying your best on the field and keep putting in performances. Hopefully, one day, the call-up might come back.”

Asked if he had heard from England manager Thomas Tuchel lately, he said: “I haven’t really spoken to him. There’s not much to say on that.”

Foden has still been on the bench for City’s last two Premier League games despite his recent improvement.

Maresca said: “We are in all competitions and we have 20 senior players – all of them very good – so there’s competition between them.

“We have so many games and many players. It’s not a short run, it’s not a sprint. It’s a marathon.

“It’s very important, for sure, in this moment to win games but it’s also very, very important to arrive in February, March, April, when things are going to be more exciting, having all of them available and fit.”

Iraola anticipates tough battle at Fenerbahce in Champions League

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Andoni Iraola believes his Liverpool side are in for a difficult test when they face Fenerbahce in the Champions League.

The Reds have made a 100% start to their league-phase campaign, defeating Atletico Madrid, LASK and Villarreal to sit third in the standings ahead of MD4.

Liverpool have also scored 12 goals this term. This is the most they have netted after three games of a European Cup/Champions League campaign since 1980-81 (also 12), a season in which they went on to lift the trophy.

However, Liverpool have not won any of their six away games against Turkish teams in the European Cup/Champions League, losing five of them (D1), and the last four in a row.

Liverpool have never lost five consecutive away games against sides from one nation in UEFA's flagship club competition, but Iraola knows a stern test awaits his side.

"It won't be easy, you talk about statistics, especially English teams playing here," said Iraola.

Andoni Iraola previews Wednesday's #UCL tie in Istanbul  https://t.co/veWMkqTawj

"They have very good players and a very good squad, they know how to play in this competition. We have to start strong in the game.

"They have very good players in midfield, they have a very good technical level. If we allow them a lot of time on the ball and don't press them properly, we will suffer.

"We need to not allow them to win confidence as they have experienced players who know how to play in this competition."

Fener and Liverpool will meet for the first time in this match – the Reds have only won four of their 11 games against Turkish sides in the European Cup/Champions League (D2 L5), although the most recent victory was a 4-0 triumph over Galatasaray in March this year.

Following their draw against Slavia Prague last time out (2-2), Fener are now on their longest-ever winless run in the Champions League (10 games – D4 L6).

Fabregas warns Como to expect ‘wild’ Lens atmosphere

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Cesc Fabregas warned his Como side they will be facing a "wild atmosphere" at Lens in the Champions League on Wednesday.

Former Spain international Fabregas played at Stade Bollaert-Delelis during his playing days when he represented Monaco.

The ex-Arsenal, Barcelona and Chelsea man will return there as head coach of Como, who have won two and lost one in Europe's premier competition this season – including a resounding 4-1 victory over Manchester United last time out.

And the 39-year-old wants his team to be prepared for a highly charged setting against the Ligue 1 outfit.

Speaking to Sky Sport Italia, Fabregas said: "It's a wild atmosphere; I played here two or three times for Monaco.

"The team is doing well and is full of energy. This is the fourth Champions League match in our history, and we're continuing to grow and gain valuable experience." Como 1907 head coach Cesc Fàbregas spoke to the media at today's press conference ahead of tomorrow's UEFA Champions League Matchday 4 fixture against RC Lens at the Stade Bollaert-Delelis. He reflected on the team's growing identity and the excitement of taking on another special European challenge.

"I love nights like this, because you can feel the passion even during the warm-up.

"It will be interesting and a marvellous experience for our players. We wanted these nights, so if you are not motivated to play these, then you're in the wrong job."

Fabregas pinpointed Florian Thauvin as a particular threat to his Como side.

The experienced winger previously played in Serie A for Udinese, before returning to France with Lens in August 2025.

"Thauvin is an incredible player, he was at Marseille, then we faced him for Udinese," Fabregas added.

"He makes the difference, has huge quality, scores a lot of goals, so he is the reference point for this team, and we need to keep a close eye on him."

Lens have gone 16 Champions League matches without a clean sheet, since a 1-0 win at the old Wembley against Arsenal in November 1998.

In European Cup/Champions League history since 1955, the only French sides with a longer run are Monaco (23, 2016-24) and Marseille (18, 2012-22).

Como, meanwhile, have scored 10 goals in their first three Champions League matches, the most ever by an Italian side in their first three European Cup games (since 1955) and most by any side since Club Brugge scored 12 in 1973.

Arteta backs Saliba’s readiness to return for Arsenal at Slavia Prague

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Defender travelled with squad after long injury absence

‘I have clips’: Arteta hits back after Liverpool complaints

Mikel Arteta is confident that William Saliba is ready to make his first appearance of the season for Arsenal after he played 45 minutes of a match behind closed doors last week.

The France defender started training on his own last month after the back injury he sustained at the World Cup but has since resumed full training and on Tuesday travelled with Arsenal’s squad to face Slavia Prague. It remains to be seen whether Saliba will feature in the Czech capital given such a lengthy absence, although Arteta insisted that he has no doubts the 25-year-old is primed to return.

Asked whether he would treat his comeback with caution, the Arsenal manager said: “Yes, but now you are in, or not. And now he’s in, and he’s in like anybody else. All the preparation has been done to measure all the loads and all the steps that he had to do to minimise that risk.

“Now he’s in a really good place. He’s built the capacity that we needed, and now it’s about giving him minutes. He’s a centre-back, and when that’s the case, it’s not like an attacking midfielder or a nine that you can change. A centre-back normally has to be able to play 90 minutes, and that’s why all the preparation that we’ve done to build him.”

Ezri Konsa and Gabriel Magalhães were outstanding in Arsenal’s landmark victory over Liverpool at Anfield on Sunday and Arteta admitted that Saliba’s return is likely to give him a selection headache. Konsa is also capable of playing at right-back, although he will face competition from Ben White and Jurriën Timber.

The latter escaped punishment against Liverpool when he pulled Alexander Isak’s shirt inside the penalty area and Arteta hit back at suggestions that officials could try to balance out that decision in future.

“I have a lot of clips about shirts, pulls, grabs. So I have a massive library that we can talk about,” Arteta said in an echo of his comments last month about straying outside his technical area. “We’re talking about one in particular. I have a massive library that we can throw there to remind everybody what every team is going through.”

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Max Dowman is also in contention to play against Slavia as he serves a three-match domestic suspension for his red card against Fleetwood last week. He became the first 15-year-old to feature in the Champions League against the same opponents 12 months ago and could break Cesc Fàbregas’s record as the youngest Arsenal player to start in the competition.

“That’s a chapter that is gone. It’s a moment of learning from all of us, and we move on,” Arteta said of Dowman’s dismissal against Fleetwood. “He’s been engaged. Today we spent some time together, talking about things in training. He’s always super positive. When you’re 16 you have a different way of looking at things and treating things, and that’s what he brings to the team as well.”

Report: Julian Ward has decided, massive Liverpool deal could now actually happen

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A huge decision has been made, and now a massive Liverpool deal could actually be happening.

Being a sporting director comes with a lot of pressures in modern football. It was a role that didn't necessarily exist in this form two decades ago.

But now, the sporting director is probably the most important position at a football club. They have to make the big decisions when it comes to the club's recruitment. They decide who and when to get rid of the coaching department and oversee numerous staff within the club.

It's an unrelenting job that also comes with a lot of scrutiny in the media.

Richard Hughes experienced that first-hand, with many proclaiming him as the reason for Liverpool's failures. His successor, Julian Ward, will want to run things differently.

Julian Ward's big decisions at LiverpoolComing into this role at this particular time makes things even more difficult for Ward. Liverpool are embarking on a huge change both on the pitch and off it. FSG have just sold a minority stake to the 1892 Holdings consortium led by Amit Bhatia. There is an option to convert that into a majority stake in the coming years ahead. With the likes of Jeff Bezos involved, there is a strong possibility that Liverpool could come under new leadership in the near future. At the same time, the squad is undergoing a metamorphosis. Liverpool are rebuilding their team after a successful decade under Jurgen Klopp. Ward's task will be to recruit the key building blocks of this rebuild and ensure Liverpool bring in the right players in order to put together a team ready to compete at the upper echelons of world football.

Coming into this role at this particular time makes things even more difficult for Ward.

Liverpool are embarking on a huge change both on the pitch and off it. FSG have just sold a minority stake to the 1892 Holdings consortium led by Amit Bhatia.

There is an option to convert that into a majority stake in the coming years ahead. With the likes of Jeff Bezos involved, there is a strong possibility that Liverpool could come under new leadership in the near future.

At the same time, the squad is undergoing a metamorphosis. Liverpool are rebuilding their team after a successful decade under Jurgen Klopp.

Ward's task will be to recruit the key building blocks of this rebuild and ensure Liverpool bring in the right players in order to put together a team ready to compete at the upper echelons of world football.

Liverpool to make a contract renewal offer for Virgil van DijkAt the same time, Ward is also going to have to make key decisions over contract renewals. Liverpool have three key players with expiring deals at the end of this season. One of them is Van Dijk. However, he is not alone. Alisson Becker's contract is also expiring, and Joe Gomez's deal is running out, too. Liverpool may be more open to letting the latter two go considering their long service at Anfield. However, Van Dijk is another matter. While there is a case to make for Gomez and Alisson to have declined in recent years, Van Dijk is still playing at an extremely high level. It's why it comes as no surprise that TEAMtalk are now reporting that Liverpool are open to making a contract renewal offer of 12-months for Van Dijk. Ward has clearly decided he would like the Dutchman to stay at Anfield. So, this massive deal could now happen. Although, it's not yet guaranteed.

Manchester United and Chelsea are reportedly interested in signing Como defender Jacobo Ramon as both clubs look to strengthen their defensive options ahead of next summer’s transfer window.

According to a report from Football Insider, the Premier League rivals have been impressed by the 21-year-old’s development in Italy. However, Real Madrid are currently in the strongest position to secure his services.

The Spanish defender has enjoyed an impressive rise since leaving Real Madrid for Como in 2025, establishing himself as an important member of Cesc Fabregas’ squad.

His performances have attracted considerable attention, with Manchester United viewing him as a potential long-term solution to their defensive problems. The Red Devils are looking to reshape their backline, and Ramon’s technical ability, composure in possession and defensive qualities could make him an attractive addition.

Chelsea are also monitoring his situation as they continue their strategy of recruiting promising young players with significant development potential.

Ramon recently strengthened his reputation by scoring against Manchester United during Como’s impressive 4-1 Champions League victory. His performances have also encouraged his former club to consider bringing him back to the Spanish capital.

Want more CaughtOffside coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust.

However, convincing Como to let the defender go could prove difficult for interested Premier League clubs.

Real Madrid have included a buy-back clause in their agreement with the Italian side, allowing them to re-sign their former academy prospect for approximately €9 million next summer.

The Spanish giants are reportedly prepared to activate that option if Ramon maintains his impressive progress throughout the campaign.

This puts United and Chelsea at a significant disadvantage, with neither club likely to match the favourable conditions available to Real Madrid.

Both Premier League sides could continue monitoring his development, but their chances of securing his signature will depend heavily on the Spanish club’s intentions.

With Ramon emerging as one of Europe’s most exciting young defenders, his future could become a major talking point during next summer’s transfer window.

AC Milan, Inter: how much will the new Milan stadium bring in?

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

This is a major announcement for the future of AC Milan and Inter. While the two Milanese clubs are preparing the construction of their new stadium, intended to replace the historic San Siro, the name of the future venue is now known. Presented during a press conference organized at the Sforza Castle in Milan, the project officially revealed its main partner: the new stadium will be called “ Generali Stadium », named after the Italian insurance giant. A particularly lucrative commercial agreement since, according to the Italian press, the naming contract should bring in around 650 million euros over a period of twenty years, or an average of 32.5 million euros per season. La Gazzetta Dello Sport. A considerable financial windfall for the two Lombard giants, who are seeking to develop their revenues in order to compete with the greatest European powers. Generali will also become the official partner of the two clubs in the insurance sector.

Scheduled for the 2031-2032 season, the inauguration of this new enclosure will mark an important stage in the history of Milanese football. The future stadium will present an original architecture, with a hexagonal exterior structure and stands arranged in a bowl, raised on a podium. The Generali name will be widely visible on the facade of the building. However, a particularity is planned for European competitions: in accordance with UEFA rules concerning the commercial rights of speakers, the Generali Stadium will take the name ” Milan Stadium » during Champions League matches and other international competitions. An operation already applied to several large European stadiums, notably those of Juventus and Arsenal. With this partnership, Milan and Inter join the list of Italian clubs having sold the naming rights to their venue, like the Allianz Stadium in Turin or Atalanta’s New Balance Arena.

Ex-scout shares verdict on Arsenal target Junior Kroupi leaving Bournemouth in January

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Eli Junior Kroupi is more likely to leave Bournemouth next summer than he is in January.

That is the opinion of experienced former Aston Villa, Tottenham and Everton scout, Bryan King, speaking exclusively to Football Insider.

The 20-year-old attracted huge interest when he scored 13 Premier League goals last season, before a significant foot injury ruled out any potential summer move.

Kroupi has now returned to action and interest has begun to mount once more, with Arsenal among the most heavily-linked suitors.

The France youth international is under contract until 2030, with the Cherries under no pressure to sell him yet.

Marco Rose will hope that keeping hold of the talented forward will boost Bournemouth's chances in both the Premier League and the Europa League this season.

"Junior and me, we like to dance." 🕺Cold assist. Cold finish. Cold cele. 🥶 pic.twitter.com/3IpeeyBTrm

Arsenal will not be alone in their pursuit of Kroupi, should they attempt to sign him in January or next summer.

The Frenchman is also a target for European champions PSG, as well as several other European powerhouses.

"Well, they've certainly made an awful lot of money. Why should he leave in January? The kid was injured," King told Football Insider.

"He had an operation in the summer. I think they're bringing him back slowly, not rushing him back, because they certainly don't want to antagonise that injury that he got.

"It'll be slowly, slowly with him, but he showed what he could do, certainly against Leeds.

"I wouldn't have any intent in selling him in January at all. I'd want him to get back to full fitness and let's see his full potential at Bournemouth and then maybe look in the summer, see how the finances are.

"'We've brought him in reasonably. If he continues to progress as he is, he's going to bring us a lot of money.'"

Decision-makers at Bournemouth will be well aware of the growing number of rivals sniffing around their brightest assets.

Not just Kroupi, but Alex Scott is expected to leave in the coming months with Chelsea reportedly leading the race.

Antonio Silva, Adrien Truffert, Marcus Tavernier and Rayan are also attracting interest ahead of what could be a difficult year for the Cherries.

Bournemouth know they cannot keep all of their stars, but they will hope to squeeze as much as possible out of them.

Marathoner Dies of Rhabdomyolysis: What to Know About Rare Condition

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Rhabdomyolysis, which caused the death of NYC marathoner Rachel Apa on Nov. 1, is a relatively rare condition, but here's what to know about what it is, who's at risk and how it becomes fatal.

When you think about athletes, you tend to think about stamina, strength and being in peak physical condition.

But anyone pushing his or her body to its limits also has to beware of overdoing it. The tragic death of Rachel Apa, a former college athlete who collapsed two miles from the finish line of the New York City Marathon, is an extreme case of a body fatally breaking down within a few hours, but a reminder that it can happen.

According to her father Steve Apa, Rachel died of rhabdomyolysis, a condition in which damaged muscle tissue breaks down and sends harmful proteins into the bloodstream.

“It’s unfathomable,” he told the New York Times. “Perfect health, perfect shape, 24 years old.”

Rachel, running her first marathon, had texted her dad after passing the 9-mile mark of the 26.2-mile route, which starts on Staten Island and finishes in Central Park, "I feel amazing. I've never felt this good."

Steve texted back from the family's home in Lewisberry, Penn., per NBC News, "Great, don’t overdo it. First marathon. This is a learning run. Don’t overdo it.”

Rachel had played field hockey for Bucknell University, was a regular exerciser and had trained for months ahead of the event.

“She was always on the go, always go," Steve said, "and it doesn’t matter if it’s school or sports or whatever it is. She was go."

So, how the heck does something like this happen?

Here is what to know about rhabdomyolysis, who is most at risk and more:

If you had never heard of rhabdomyolysis (or "rhabdo," as it's often abbreviated) you're hardly alone, as it's considered relatively rare, with about 26,000 cases reported in the United States each year, meaning it affects .0076 percent of the country's population.

According to the National Institutes of Health, rhabdomyolysis is a condition in which skeletal muscles break down and release intracellular components such as myoglobin, creatine phosphokinase (CPK), electrolytes and sarcoplasmic proteins into the bloodstream.

When toxic components of your muscle fibers enter your circulation system and kidneys, it can lead to acute kidney injury (AKI), electrolyte disturbances, and disseminated intravascular coagulation (a blood clotting disorder), per the NIH.

A "crushing injury" is the most common cause of rhabdomyolysis, according to the Cleveland Clinic, while overexertion (too much exercise without rest) and severe dehydration and overheating can also cause rapid muscle breakdown.

Cited causes of rhabdomyolysis also include certain medications, such as antipsychotic, antidepressant and antiviral medications, as well as statins; long periods of inactivity and physical conditions such as muscular dystrophy.

Endurance athletes and members of the military—i.e. those who engage in high-intensity physical activity—are considered more at risk, per the Cleveland Clinic, as are firefighters and others who work in hot environments and people over 65, as they're considered more prone to falls that could lead to injuries and prolonged immobility.

“Exertional rhabdomyolysis can occur after strenuous exercise and from high-intensity workouts during which the muscles are overused,” Niloofar Nobakht, MD, associate clinical professor of nephrology, told UCLA Health in 2023. “You can also get rhabdo from direct trauma, such as a crushing injury from a motor vehicle accident or a fall.”

But almost any intense activity can cause rhabdo, most commonly among people who are trying something new, according to Eric Rawson, chair of the department of health, nutrition and exercise science at Messiah College in Pennsylvania.

"You can be fit, and I can come up with a workout that you are unaccustomed to, and that could be what causes rhabdo," he told the NY Times in 2017, explaining why it's so important to start out at a beginner's level of a new activity before notching up the intensity.

Experiencing sore or weak muscles a few days after exercising, muscle swelling and abnormally dark or otherwise off-color urine can be signs of the condition.

The Cleveland Clinic notes that symptoms usually develop one to three days after a muscle injury, and persistent soreness doesn't always register as a potential life-threatening complication.

Rhabdomyolysis is survivable but requires prompt treatment, according to medical experts. Usually the condition is treated with fluids and electrolytes administered intravenously, while extreme kidney damage could require dialysis.

“We might start with an IV fluid and closely monitor electrolytes such as potassium, phosphorus and calcium," UCLA's Nobakht said. "Potassium and calcium are the two main electrolytes that play a significant role in recovering muscle function."

Witnesses said that Rachel Apa collapsed around the 24-mile mark of the New York City Marathon in Central Park. Three people, two of whom said they were doctors, took turns administering CPR, spectator Michaela Nicholas told the NY Times. New York Road Runners, which organizes the marathon, said event medical staff used a defibrillator on the fallen athlete before paramedics arrived and transported her to Mount Sinai Medical Center, where she was pronounced dead after six hours of attempted life-saving measures.

University of Michigan Health emergency medicine physician Dr. Bradley Uren told the publication that, for Rachel, "that worst case scenario happened just very, very quickly."

US Army Black Hawk Helicopter Nearly Struck By Fireworks During Dramatic NFL Flyover

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A US Army Black Hawk helicopter narrowly avoided a potentially dangerous encounter with stadium fireworks during a pregame flyover at an NFL game between the Seattle Seahawks and Chicago Bears on November 2, 2026. The incident occurred at Lumen Field in Seattle, Washington, when pyrotechnics erupted while the final helicopter in a four-aircraft formation was still passing over the stadium. Dramatic footage captured the fireworks exploding remarkably close to the military aircraft, prompting audible gasps from spectators.

The flyover was part of the national anthem ceremony ahead of the Monday Night Football matchup, which attracted approximately 70,000 spectators. Although military helicopter appearances are a familiar feature of major sporting events in the United States, the incident raised questions about coordination between stadium personnel and military flight crews. Fortunately, the helicopter continued flying after the fireworks erupted, with no apparent damage reported.

Four US Army Sikorsky UH-60M Black Hawk helicopters from the 2-158th Assault Helicopter Battalion, part of the 16th Combat Aviation Brigade, performed the ceremonial flyover at Lumen Field. The aircraft approached the stadium in formation as part of the pregame celebrations before the Seahawks faced the Chicago Bears. According to MyNorthwest, the first three helicopters passed over the stadium before the fireworks display began. However, the fourth aircraft was still nearby when the stadium's pyrotechnics were activated.

Video footage shared widely on social media shows fireworks launching from the stadium structure and exploding extremely close to the trailing helicopter. From certain camera angles, the pyrotechnics appear to pass alongside the aircraft's rear section, creating the impression that the helicopter might have been struck. The aircraft nevertheless continued its flight behind the other three helicopters, with no visible signs of damage.

The incident was also reported by the New York Post, which highlighted the frightening proximity of the fireworks and the reactions from spectators. While some footage appears to show fireworks passing directly alongside the aircraft, it remains unclear whether any projectile actually made contact. Neither an official explanation for the timing nor confirmation of an aircraft inspection was available in the initial reporting.

Fireworks are generally not compatible with aircraft operating in close proximity, especially during low-altitude helicopter demonstrations. Although a military helicopter such as the UH-60M is designed for demanding operating environments, its rotors, engines, flight controls, and other components remain vulnerable to foreign objects and external damage. A firework exploding near a helicopter could potentially expose its crew to burning debris, heat, and fragments, while also creating a distraction during a maneuver that requires precise aircraft control.

Sikorsky, part of Lockheed Martin, manufactures the Black Hawk, a twin-engine military utility helicopter. Its design emphasizes operational versatility, survivability, and the ability to continue flying under challenging conditions. However, these characteristics should not be mistaken for immunity to airborne hazards. Even a relatively small incident involving a rotor blade or an engine intake could have serious consequences, particularly when an aircraft is operating close to a crowded venue.

A historical example also shows why coordination between aircraft operators and pyrotechnics teams is essential. On July 23, 1982, a Bell UH-1 helicopter crashed in California during the filming of Twilight Zone: The Movie after special-effects explosions detonated too close to the aircraft. The accident killed actor Vic Morrow and two child actors, and injured six other people. According to the National Transportation Safety Board's investigation, debris and heat from the explosions damaged the helicopter's rotor blades, causing the tail rotor assembly to separate and the helicopter to descend uncontrollably. The investigation identified inadequate communication and coordination between the helicopter pilot and film director as contributing factors.

The army has several installations in the DC area.

Despite the potential dangers, aircraft demonstrations and pyrotechnics can be combined in the same organized event when appropriate safety procedures are in place. Airshows regularly combine military aircraft demonstrations, ground-based explosions, smoke effects, and other theatrical elements to recreate combat scenarios or enhance performances, and we have personally witnessed many such impressive performances, including the legendary one at Oshkosh. The key distinction is that these activities require careful planning, clearly defined operating areas, and communication between participating teams. Aircraft must follow predetermined flight paths that keep them away from designated pyrotechnic zones.

The Federal Aviation Administration ( FAA) provides specific guidance for organizing aviation events involving ground-based pyrotechnics. Its airshow safety requirements emphasize documenting pyrotechnic device locations, establishing appropriate public-protection procedures, and coordinating ground operations. The agency also highlights the importance of separating aircraft movements from fireworks displays. Its guidance on mass aircraft demonstrations further stresses the need for detailed flight plans, mandatory briefings, and geographical, vertical, lateral, and time separation between participating aircraft.

For stadium flyovers, timing is everything because helicopters generally fly slower than many fixed-wing aircraft, so they can stay near a venue longer. This makes coordination between flight crews, stadium production teams, and pyrotechnics operators especially important. In Seattle, the fireworks appeared to begin before the final Black Hawk had completely cleared the stadium area. Although the helicopter continued flying without any apparent consequences, the incident demonstrated how a seemingly routine pregame celebration can create an unnecessary aviation hazard.

Enzo Maresca: ‘We cannot rely on Erling for every game’

Phil Foden is aiming for an England recall after the Manchester City attacker was left out of the squad for last summer’s World Cup and the Nations League games this season by Thomas Tuchel.

While the next round of England games in the competition – the squad for which will be named on Friday – may come too soon for a recall, Foden wants to force his way back into Tuchel’s plans. The 26-year-old said: “Hopefully, one day the call-up might come back. It’s one of those things [being dropped]. Football is full of ups and downs. You have to try your best, keep putting on performances.”

Foden revealed Tuchel has not been in touch since the World Cup to discuss what the manager wishes to see from him. “No, I haven’t really spoken to him to be honest, so not much to say on that,” he said.

In September’s 1-0 win at Manchester United Foden was sent off after 23 minutes, resulting in a three-match ban. As this came a week before the last international window, he did not play again for more than a month.

Foden said: “Obviously, I had the unfortunate red against United but the break did me good, I came back fresh and sharp. I’ve been involved in a lot of goals recently and I’m happy. Personally [the season] – it’s gone really well. I took some family time [after the red card], went on holiday. It was a very long time, which felt strange. It gave me time to work on things I need to work on. I felt like I could help the team.”

Saturday’s 1-0 loss to Brighton was the first time Maresca’s side has failed to score this season. Yet despite racking up 36 goals in all competitions, Haaland’s 13 is seven more than the next highest scorer, Rayan Cherki. Foden’s four goals – including a hat-trick in City’s last European outing, against AEK Athens – is the third best.

Maresca said: “It’s very important that in each game we create chances. For sure when you have Erling inside the pitch, it’s better because he’s going to score goals. But you know that he’s always there, close. At the same time, we cannot rely on Erling for every game.

He was asked if he can support Haaland more and produce his form of three years ago. “Hopefully,” he said. “[The key is] keep playing regularly, being happy and positive. Being in the right positions and hopefully I can get back to that level now. I’m getting there slightly more now.”

"Covert Contracts" Might Be Wrecking Your Relationship

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you’ve ever thought “after everything I do,” therapists say you might have one.

You did bedtime solo every single night this week. You did the baths, with all the splishing and the splashing and the bubble anarchy. You fielded the lightning round of existential questions at 8:47 p.m. (“Mommy, what happens to doggies when they die?”). You tucked everyone in and then did your regular closing-time routine to get the rest of the house buttoned up for bed.

So when Saturday morning rolls around, you’re planning to sleep in. You fully expect your partner to wake up and handle the kids, the breakfast, the early morning dog walking duties. The whole shebang. Tit for tat.

Then your kids are in your face before you can even pry your eyes open, wondering where their soccer cleats are or if you can get more juice. Cue the rage, right? OK, so here comes the uncomfortable gut-check… did you communicate your expectations to your partner? Or did you just sort of assume it was a done deal?

This scenario has been dubbed a "covert contract," and therapists say it can lead to a whole lot of resentment building up in a relationship over time. Here’s what experts want you to know about where this behavior comes from, how to spot it in your own actions, and how it differs from the imbalance of the invisible load.

“A covert contract is a deal only one person signed,” says Kristyn Carmichael, Esq., a family mediator, certified divorce financial analyst, and founder of Couples Solutions Center. “It sounds like ‘If I do X, you’ll do Y,’ but the other partner never saw the terms, never agreed to them, and usually doesn’t know the contract exists.”

And she often has a front-row seat to what happens when those deals go unaddressed for a prolonged period of time. “In mediation, I see the end stage of these: one spouse arrives with years of unpaid ‘invoices,’ and the other is honestly confused about what they owe.”

Cheryl Groskopf, an anxiety, trauma, and attachment therapist who authored The Somatic Healing Workbook, likens it to writing a big ol' agreement and assigning your partner an equally big role in it. Somehow, your partner is the only person unaware of said agreement.

But your partner being unaware doesn’t change the way you feel. The hurt is just as real as if they’d actually broken a promise. “In your brain, they did,” Groskopf explains.

Um, yes. One hundred percent. This was something every expert agreed on! However, the need itself isn’t the issue here; it’s the delivery method.

“The need underneath is reasonable, but if it’s not articulated overtly, then it’s unreasonable to think your partner would be a mind reader,” says Dr. Jenni Skyler, Adam & Eve’s resident sex and relationship therapist.

Already, as a busy working mom who happens to be the default parent, I instinctively start to feel myself bristle at this idea. No one tells me these things or what needs to be done — I just notice and do it because someone has to. The experts say that sort of thinking is a fast path to frustration.

“‘I want my partner to make an effort on my birthday’ is a totally reasonable desire,” Groskopf says. “‘I shouldn’t have to tell them what I want because if they really loved me, they’d already know’ is when it becomes problematic.”

Now, the experts were also abundantly clear that none of this means moms should just silently accept an unfair split.

“One person also shouldn’t have to become a household manager simply because they are more organized, quicker to notice what needs to be done, or less comfortable leaving things unfinished,” says Ilona Phillips, a psychotherapist and founder of Lotus Counseling in Ann Arbor, Michigan. “Expecting shared responsibility is reasonable. Expecting a partner to recognize an unspoken sacrifice and reciprocate exactly one-for-one can get partners in trouble.”

“If I ask for a break and you say no, that’s a direct rejection,” Carmichael says. “If I never ask, I’m protected from that, and I keep the moral high ground.” And, yes, there’s also that belief so many of us carry that “if I have to ask, it doesn’t count,” she adds, “as if a gesture only proves love when it’s unprompted.”

That belief tends to run deep, too (ask me how I know). Says Groskopf, “If you grew up learning that your needs were inconvenient, asking can feel incredibly vulnerable.” Instead of just asking, we end up creating these little litmus tests our partners don’t even know they’re taking.

She uses flowers as an example. If you ask for them, you know they showed up because you asked. But if your partner brings them on their own, "There’s this feeling that you were appreciated, that they noticed you, that you MATTER. And for someone who grew up feeling overlooked or emotionally neglected, that distinction can feel huge.”

Oof. The catch here is that this strategy often just backfires.

“There is a fear that if we directly communicate what we need, and that need is not met, we will be hurt,” explains Dr. Paulette Didia, a licensed clinical psychologist in New York City. “However, if we do not communicate what we need, our partner will not know, our partner will not do it, and we will be hurt anyway. We think hurting in our own mind ‘hurts less,’ and that is not true.”

Admittedly, the lines are a bit blurry. When you’re doing the lion’s share of invisible work in your home and family, it’s hard not to “keep score” or have a running tally in your head of what your partner did versus what you did.

Unfortunately, that scoreboard warps how both partners see each other.

“Once you develop a narrative that your partner isn’t doing enough, it becomes very easy to fill that bucket with examples of how they’re falling short. Meanwhile, your spouse feels like they’re always in the doghouse and starts to wonder if it’s even worth trying. One person is thinking, ‘Don’t you see how hard I’m working?’ while the other is thinking, ‘What did I do wrong now?’” says Mary McLaughlin, LCSW, a couples therapist and discernment counselor.

So while it’s fair to feel frustrated by an invisible (or visible) load imbalance, a little communication can go a long way. That’s true on both sides of the line. “Even if the score is unequal, if all parties know the score and acknowledge it and offer gratitude, this can offset resentment,” says Skyler.

Well, obviously this is tricky since you probably don’t know you’re making them. However, there are a few tells you can look for, according to the experts:

First and foremost, resist the urge to try settling years of “back pay.” You can’t Uno Reverse this one. Instead, focus on rewriting the terms and making sure you and your partner are, in fact, on the same page. Here are a few practical pointers from our pros on how to pull that off.

Didia asks couples to fill out a simple weekly worksheet together that includes tasks along with who does them and when. She has couples do this at the same time each week so that it doesn’t become one more thing Mom has to track, and they post the list where both can see it so no one becomes the default reminder.

If you want to follow suit, start by listing every task (big and small). Next, let each partner choose what they’ll take, and split the ones nobody wants. Very important info: The person who owns a task decides how and when it gets done. “Maybe you think this should be done by Monday, but your partner indicates it will be done by Wednesday,” Didia says. “Since it is their task, they get to decide and, by Tuesday, you will not already be annoyed.”

Phillips encourages a weekly check-in where partners take turns asking: What’s been working well between us? Is anything feeling off? Extra difficult? Frustrating as hell? Unacknowledged? And, finally, how can I be a better partner to you this week?

You may be tempted to stop doing certain things, like washing the dishes, hoping that someone will notice. That’s just a covert contract in a different outfit, says Phillips. And even if your partner does pick up the task, resentment can still linger. You might then think, Why did I have to stop before they noticed?

“Relationships change. What worked when you were first dating, before kids, 10 years ago may not work anymore,” reminds McLaughlin. “Couples need to renegotiate instead of assuming the old arrangement still works.”

When you realize you’re locked in a pattern that won’t budge, it’s probably time to see a couples therapist. Carmichael’s advice is to get help sooner rather than later. By the time couples land in her office, she says the covert contracts show up even in how they divorce, i.e., “I gave up my career, so I deserve more.”

At the end of the day, adds McLaughlin, “Resentment paired with avoidance is a particularly risky combination for couples.” And that means the fix, both annoyingly simple and incredibly hard, is saying what you need out loud… and holding each other accountable to those needs in a way that feels fair and equitable to you both.

Chelsea are reportedly interested in signing Everton midfielder Harrison Armstrong as Xabi Alonso looks to strengthen his options in the middle of the park ahead of the upcoming transfer windows.

The England Under-21 international has established himself as one of Everton’s most promising young talents, making considerable progress since breaking into the senior squad.

His technical ability, composure in possession and versatility in midfield have attracted attention from several Premier League clubs. Armstrong has already made 32 senior appearances for Everton, providing four assists, and continues to develop into an important player for David Moyes.

Chelsea are looking to invest in young players with considerable potential, and Armstrong could be an exciting long-term addition. Alonso would benefit from having another technically gifted midfielder capable of providing energy and creativity in the centre of the pitch.

However, convincing Everton to part ways with their academy graduate will not be straightforward. The Toffees previously considered a £40 million offer from Nottingham Forest during the summer, but eventually abandoned the proposed transfer following a strong reaction from supporters.

Armstrong remains under contract at Everton until 2028, putting the Merseyside club in a reasonably strong negotiating position.

The club highly regards the midfielder, and there is little indication they would welcome another approach for his services.

Chelsea are expected to continue monitoring his development through regular scouting reports. Although the youngster fits their recruitment strategy, Everton’s determination to retain him could make negotiations extremely difficult.

Don’t Forget, Oscar Watchers: ‘The Odyssey,’ ‘Project Hail Mary,’ and Other Big Guns Are Coming Back

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The fall festivals have come and gone, leaving specialty sugarplums dancing in Oscar pundits’ heads, from Netflix’s Spanish favorite “La Bola Negra” to Searchlight’s hilarious “Wild Horse Nine,” which just boasted a raucous Academy screening reception.

But don’t forget the big guns, movies of scale and scope that wowed audiences earlier in the year. Some will fill the craft categories (see “Spider-Man: Brand New Day”) or dominate Best Animated Feature (see Pixar’s “Toy Story 5,” which might land Taylor Swift an Oscar for Best Song). Others will wow not only cinephiles but the mainstream voters in the Academy. Their campaigners must push them back into voters’ consciousness, remind them of what they liked so much, all those months ago. Related Stories ‘The Intertwining’ Review: Two Medical Students Uncover Buried Truths in Tense Psychological Drama Billy Evans Hosts AMA on (His Own) X Account: ‘You Can See Everything’ Subject on Not Being a Cocaine Fan, Going Full Frontal, and What His Cousin’s Thing Was

On November 11, “Disclosure Day” Best Actress contender Emily Blunt will launch her award season push for an “Oppenheimer” follow-up Oscar nomination with a SAG Q&A in New York, followed by November 13 in LA. On November 15, she’s conducting press conferences for the Critics Choice Awards and the Golden Globes. She’ll also do rounds of press interviews. And on December 10, she’ll accept the Kirk Douglas Award for Excellence in Film from the Santa Barbara International Film Festival.

In other words, Blunt is in it to win it.

Best Shots: Truth is, Universal is pragmatically putting Blunt front and center for “Disclosure Day” for her remarkable turn as a foreign-language-spouting weatherwoman inhabited by aliens, as opposed to filmmaker Steven Spielberg or costar Josh O’Connor. Craft contenders will include five-time Oscar-winning composer John Williams, possibly up for his 55th and last Oscar nomination, two-time Oscar-winning cinematographer Janusz Kaminski, Editing, Sound, and VFX.

The well-reviewed March blockbuster has come roaring back, with filmmakers Phil Lord and Chris Miller doing most of the promo duties so far. The heart-tugging sci-fi epic produced by Amy Pascal is competing at the Golden Globes for comedy as “The Martian” did before it. The DVD released in May. “PHM” will re-release in IMAX and IMAX 70 theaters starting December 11.

Best Shots: Ryan Gosling is a Best Actor contender as scientist Ryland Grace — not a trained astronaut — who is in way over his head on a space mission to save the Earth but uses science to figure out the many problems that come his way. Gosling carries the movie but also interacts beautifully with not only his commanding mission director Sandra Hüller (who is competing with herself in Supporting Actress for “PHM” and “Fatherland”) but James Ortiz, who was on set as puppeteer and voice for another solo space traveler, a crablike alien named Rocky. He’s contending for Supporting Actor. (Whether the actors branch will accept him is a big question.)

Lord & Miller are in the mix for Best Director, and “The Martian” Oscar nominee Drew Goddard could be in the running again for Adapted Screenplay. The movie could land several craft categories as well, including VFX, Sound, Cinematography, Editing and Daniel Pemberton‘s Original Score, which supports the different tones of the movie, through action, comedy, and pathos.

Universal just released their robust screening and panel schedule for “The Odyssey,” with Christopher Nolan and his cast and key crew to be grilled by the likes of Guillermo del Toro and Robert Downey, Jr. as they step in front of Guild and Academy voters. Not coincidentally, the movie hits DVD, Blu-ray, and 4K Ultra HD on November 17.

Best Shots: “The Odyssey” is in front-runner position for Best Actor. Matt Damon has wisely leaned into the degree of difficulty required to play the peripatetic wily warrior Odysseus in harsh locations that helped Leonardo DiCaprio win for “The Revenant.” Damon is a wily and charming campaigner who is well-liked not only in Hollywood — he has worked with everyone — but around the world. (Check out his Criterion Closet Picks.) He won a Screenplay Oscar with Ben Affleck (“Good Will Hunting”) but has never won an Acting Oscar.

The ubiquitous Anne Hathaway (who won for “Les Mis”) is leading the Supporting Actress field for her performance as the patient Penelope. Constantly promoting a slew of movies this year as she became more and more pregnant did not hurt her cause. She’s competing with two-time nominee Samantha Morton, a Brit, as the witch Circe. Veteran American John Leguizamo, never nominated, leads the British “Odyssey” Supporting Actor contenders Rob Pattinson and Tom Holland.

Of this group, “The Odyssey” and “Project Hail Mary” will dominate the Oscar nominations, likely scoring from 10-15 each. “Sinners,” a period movie with original music, holds the record with 16.

Crystal Palace downfall traced back to two players by ex-chief scout

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Crystal Palace have a major weakness when it comes to leadership and set pieces which must be resolved in their upcoming games.

Pressure is building on Pierre Sage's future after a turbulent start to the season at Selhurst Park, and their weakness at defending set plays has contributed heavily.

The Eagles have conceded 21 goals injust nine Premier League games so far this season, with four of those coming via set pieces, the second-worse of any team.

Former Man United chief scout Mick Brown told Football Insider that Sage must resolve his team's frailties, but the Crystal Palace players must also take accountability.

The South London club have lost leaders including Marc Guehi and Maxence Lacroix in the last 12 months, which has weakened them on the pitch but also mentally.

Lacking those key influences and players who are willing to take responsibility is now costing Palace, particularly from set pieces where they are especially weak.

Football Insider revealed on 25 October that Crystal Palace's failure to adequately replace the likes of Guehi, Lacroix and Daniel Munoz came as a significant blow to Sage.

The lack of leadership in the back line following Lacroix's exit has become a major concern, with loanees Axel Disasi and Honest Ahanor having struggled so far.

That lack of accountability and leadership has been evident not just in Palace's shocking defensive record, but also on set pieces, where they have been urged to step up.

"Crystal Palace have been terribly defensively, and now they're struggling from set pieces too," Brown told Football Insider.

"Obviously the finger will be pointed at the manager, but the players have got to step up and take some accountability in these situations to deal with the issue themselves.

"The big thing for Crystal Palace, when you look at it, is they've lost players like Marc Guehi and Maxence Lacroix who were real leaders at the back.

"They would have no problem taking control of a situation, taking responsibility, and making sure they dealt with conrners, free kicks, anything like that.

"Looking at this Palace squad now, which of those players are going to step up and take control, take accountability, and deal with things? It's becoming a major weakness.

"Like I said, the blame will come back on the manager and maybe they could be better prepared for these situations, but it's the players who have been letting him down.

"They're in desperate need of leaders at Crystal Palace, and my big worry for them is that these players don't seem to be showing up when they are so badly needed."

Demands for Crystal Palace defenders and leaders to step up have come alongside ongoing speculation about Sage's future in the job, with his position under threat.

The Frenchman has come under fire in recent weeks following a string of disappointing results which have left the Eagles in the bottom three in the Premier League table.

Brown told Football Insider on 2 November that Crystal Palace's star names are still in support of the manager but must start to show that support on the pitch.

Reports have recently claimed that senior players have sent messages of support to the manager as they reaffirmed their backing despite recent results going against them.

However, those players have now been called upon to step up on the pitch, with many having let Sage down and contributed directly to the pressure via their performances.

Carrick: ‘Roma are a really good team, big game for Manchester United’

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Michael Carrick admits Roma ‘are a really good team’ and will put Manchester United under pressure in the Champions League tonight, as he explains some unusual choices.

It kicks off at Old Trafford at 20.00 GMT (21.00 CEST).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from this game and Feyenoord vs. Inter on the Liveblog.

Lenny Yoro is available again after his suspension against Como, but Benjamin Sesko, Patrick Dorgu, Marcus Rashford, Manuel Ugarte and Luke Shaw are still out of action.

“It’s a big game, and it’s what you expect in this competition. [Roma] have had a really good start to the season and credit to them for that, they’re a really good team. It’s why we want to be in this competition, we want to challenge ourselves against good teams, and try to come out on top. It’s a big night for us,” Carrick told Amazon Prime UK via BBC Sport.

Lisandro Martinez was sent off at the weekend in the defeat to Chelsea and is dropped to the bench today.

“We have to pick a team. We have a good squad, we have decisions to make. We have changed one or two in recent weeks, so we made the decision to go with this team tonight.”

Kobbie Mainoo made his comeback from injury as a substitute over the weekend, and starts today with Carlos Baleba for a new partnership.

“We have some really good midfielders, and different combinations provide different strengths. Andrey Santos and Carlos Baleba have played recently together. Kobbie is back fit, and we just felt it was a good stage to put him back in.

“You’d like to think that they complement each other with the attributes they both have, and I’m looking forward to seeing them tonight,” explained Carrick.

Manchester United have been on disastrous form, without a victory since the 4-0 Champions League result with Sabah FK on September 10.

That was followed by three draws and six defeats in all competition, including a crushing 4-1 loss on Italian soil against Como.

On the other hand, Roma lost only one game all season, 4-3 to Real Madrid when leading 3-1 going into the 82nd minute.

“They play man to man and press all over the pitch you never really get time on the ball. Their play never really settles. Roma are really good at what they do.”

Carrick was not only in the Manchester United side that beat Roma 7-1 in 2007, he also scored two goals alongside Cristiano Ronaldo.

“That’s a long time ago. I think tonight is a whole new Manchester United. It’s the next opportunity for us to win a football match, we are looking to do that, we need to do that, and tonight would be a good one to win,” he concluded.

Weston McKennie remains in doubt for Juventus’ away game against AZ Alkmaar due to muscle fatigue, but coach Luciano Spalletti will decide his availability on Wednesday.

Juventus may have to cope without McKennie in an away Europa League game against AZ Alkmaar on Thursday.

The Bianconeri have earned seven points in the opening three Europa League games of the season.

Update Emerges on Mo Salah’s Life in Turkey After 14 Trabzonspor Games

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

An interesting new update has emerged on Mo Salah's life in Turkey, just days after he made his 10th league appearance for Trabzonspor since leaving Liverpool in the summer.

The Egyptian King spent nine years at Anfield, winning the Premier League title twice and helping the Reds claim their sixth Champions League crown.

Even last season, many believed the now-34-year-old still had plenty left in the tank to continue playing at the highest level. Following his emotional announcement, Jamie Carragher suggested he would surely end up somewhere like Napoli or AC Milan in Serie A.

Instead, Salah opted for Turkey, but not with the usual suspects such as Galatasaray or Besiktas. He chose Trabzonspor, a club not typically expected to challenge for major honours, but one whose ambitions have been raised by his arrival.

If Salah's 2026/27 season could be summed up in one saying, it would have to be 'the gift that keeps on giving'. The Egyptian continues to defy Father Time, consistently proving himself to be the gold standard for modern footballers, having scored eight goals and provided a further three assists in 10 Super Lig matches.

Salah has directly contributed to more than 45 per cent of his side's league goals and currently tops the goalscoring charts, with Trabzonspor sitting third in the table, above the likes of Fenerbahce and just three points behind the league leaders. Watch his highlights of his most recent appearance in a 2-0 win over Gaziantep below:

Mo Salah's statistics in Trabzonspor 2-0 Gaziantep

He has also registered two goals and two assists in two Europa Conference League appearances. Having scored and assisted in 13 European matches since the start of the 2017/18 season, he has extended his record for the most matches with both a goal and an assist in UEFA competitions, with his closest rival trailing by four.

With an assist in a 3-0 win away to Samsunspor in October, Salah also became the first player to reach 10 goal involvements in just seven Super Lig games since records began, setting the bar yet again and showing no signs of slowing down.

Mohamed Salah is earning a fortune at Trabzonspor.

According to Mohamed Kaoud, a journalist for BBC Arabic, Salah is to be awarded Turkish citizenship alongside his family.

He is also poised to receive the State Medal for Distinguished Service - a high-level honour to reflect an "unprecedented surge" in the sports and tourism sectors in Turkey in recent times. On Facebook, he wrote:

"I have learned that Mohamed Salah will 'definitely' be granted Turkish citizenship. Officials from Trabzon are currently working with Turkish state authorities to expedite the naturalisation process for the Egyptian football icon, as well as his wife and their three children.

"Furthermore, Salah is set to be awarded the 'State Medal for Distinguished Service' during a major ceremony, and will receive a high-level honour in recognition of his contribution to an unprecedented surge in both the sports and tourism sectors in Turkey - all achieved in record time."

The Premier League has seen an abundance of extremely talented players, but who makes the cut?

In a response, Masrawy, citing a source, claimed that “there has been no official contact with Salah from Turkish officials regarding his obtaining Türkiye citizenship" but clarified that he is set to meet with Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan in the near future.

His arrival at Trabzonspor has been seismic - with a reported 116,000 shirts sold as of late September. The 152% increase brought in around £8 million in revenue, with Turkiye Today reporting that Salah takes 20 per cent of the funds on products with his name on.

Fantasy Football studs & duds Week 9 of 2026 NFL season

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The unstoppable machine that is the NFL season continues on for another week. The key is to get ahead of the bye weeks and the disappointing performances while maximizing the good ones. All we can do is move forward, since the NFL changes like the wind. So, let's dig into some fantasy football studs and duds for Week 9.

MORE: 3 teams that should be worried after Week 8

Oct 19, 2026; Santa Clara, California, USA; San Francisco 49ers quarterback Brock Purdy (13) throws a pass during the first quarter Washington Commanders at Levi's Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Cary Edmondson-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

There's no doubt that Purdy is a better quarterback than Geno Smith. After Smith threw for 290 yards and two touchdowns against the Raiders last week, what is Purdy going to do against them in Week 9?

Coming out of the bye week should benefit the 49ers as, once again, injuries haven't been kind to them. However, with Mike Evans and Deebo Samuel out for the entire game, and Christian McCaffrey for a quarter, Purdy still threw for 288 yards and a touchdown in Week 7.

Evans and Samuel should be healthy, and the Raiders are allowing the sixth most points to opposing quarterbacks this season. Despite the injuries, Purdy still has 1,748 passing yards and 15 touchdowns through seven games. Things are lining up well for him this week.

Let's keep the good times going for Brown. While the matchup might be slightly better for him this week against the Atlanta Falcons, I can't imagine the Bengals are going to score 43 points again in Week 9. However, both the production and the role were positive developments.

Brown finished with 55 yards on 12 carries while catching eight passes for 46 yards. Seeing his role in the passing game with nine targets bodes well for his future outlook. He's up to 36 catches for 199 yards on the season, as that adds a nice floor to Brown's expectations.

With Kyler Murray's status for Week 9 in doubt, it might be beneficial for Jefferson if Carson Wentz is back under center. Either way, the superstar receiver is set up for success this week against the Buffalo Bills.

Yes, the Vikings have a strong defense, but the Bills offense is equally as good, so Minnesota will have to keep pace. However, Buffalo allows the most fantasy points to opposing wide receivers, so Jefferson should be in position to feast all game. And if Wentz is throwing him the ball, even better.

Jan 26, 2025; Philadelphia, PA, USA; Washington Commanders quarterback Jayden Daniels (5) looks to pass the ball against the Philadelphia Eagles during the second half in the NFC Championship game at Lincoln Financial Field. Mandatory Credit: Eric Hartline-Imagn Images

Jayden Daniels, QB, Washington Commanders

While Daniels is back, the Commanders are being careful with him, and he's not running. Last week, he carried the ball just two times for 23 yards and failed to generate much upside. He did generate some offense through the air with 217 yards and a touchdown, but things should be tougher in Week 9 against the Los Angeles Rams.

Not having Terry McLaurin doesn't help, but the larger issue here is the fact that the Commanders appear to be cautious in deploying Daniels on the ground. It makes sense as they want to keep him healthy, but it does limit the upside. And the Rams aren't going to make things easy for him.

Lamar Jackson was back, and then he wasn't. He went to Injured Reserve with a thumb injury, and then Tyler Huntley went down as well. The Ravens were down to their third-string quarterback, and the results were as expected.

It's unclear if Huntley will be back under center in Week 9, but regardless, it will be a tough matchup against the Jacksonville Jaguars this week. They allow the second fewest fantasy points to opposing running backs, and Henry is in a less-than-ideal situation. After three weeks without a touchdown, Henry reached the end zone in Week 8, but he had only 11 carries for 53 yards.

I'm not sure that he'll get many more carries this week, and when he does, room to run will be hard to come by.

McMillan was targeted 11 times last week, but it translated into only six catches for 58 yards. Yes, McMillan has big play potential at all times, and he's had explosive success, but he's also inconsistent and prone to struggles in difficult matchups. Facing the Denver Broncos and Patrick Surtain this week certainly qualifies.

Kim Hellberg drops new Will Lankshear update in big Middlesbrough boost

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kim Hellberg has eased fears regarding a potential injury issue at Middlesbrough for star striker Will Lankshear.

The forward has been a stunning addition to the Teesside outfit from Tottenham Hotspur over the summer, making a big splash in these opening months of the new Championship season.

The 21-year-old found the back of the net on the 12th and 13th occasions of the campaign in the team’s 3-0 victory against Charlton Athletic last weekend.

However, he was substituted in the closing stages, and was seen limping off the field, which raised worries among supporters that he could be facing an absence through injury.

Lankshear arrived from Spurs in a deal worth a total value of £20 million, with the club hoping he can play a role in helping the team gain promotion to the Premier League.

Hellberg’s side lost the play-off final last May, but their positive form in the first 13 rounds of this season have them in the mix for a top two position so far.

Speaking ahead of Middlesbrough’s upcoming clash away to Wrexham, Hellberg assured supporters that Lankshear’s withdrawal was just due to cramp.

According to journalist Dom Shaw, the Boro boss confirmed that the forward was taken off with the issue, and he should be available again midweek.

Southampton and Middlesbrough were set to be handed blows in January - but not anymore

However, Rokas Pukstas will be absent for at least another month, with no specific timeframe given for his potential return to fitness.

The midfielder arrived at the Riverside in the summer from Hajduk Split, but has only made one appearance in the Championship.

He came off the bench in the 1-0 win against QPR in September, but has been unavailable for selection due to a hamstring injury suffered in training in October.

Alfie Jones is the only other absence, with the defender still recovering from a calf issue.

Middlesbrough are currently top of the Championship table after the opening 13 games, with the team earning 28 points.

Hellberg’s side are two points clear of Swansea City in second, and another two clear of third place Wolves, as they target a top two finish in the league.

Boro have lost just once, and are unbeaten since their 2-1 defeat to Blackburn Rovers on 22 August, going 11 without a loss.

Next up for Middlesbrough is a trip to face Wrexham on Wednesday evening in a 7.45pm kick-off.

Lankshear has been sensational this season, and his brace at the weekend was yet another reminder of his importance to the club already.

So fans are going to be extra wary of any signs of a possible injury because of how significant his form has been.

It’s a positive sign for Wednesday night that he should be fit and ready to go, although it’s becoming apparent that he can’t quite manage 90 minutes every game.

It wouldn’t be a surprise that he only features for some of this clash against the Red Dragons, but being able to get him in from the start will be a relief for supporters.

Ben Johnson reacts to Bears' statement win over Seahawks

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Chicago Bears' 34-17 win over the Seattle Seahawks on Monday Night Football was a statement win for this team. However, Bears head coach Ben Johnson was not looking to make a statement since this team is still growing.

"When the pressure's high, we want to be at our best. And all three phases did that," Johnson said. "We didn't come here to make a statement, we're still building."

This may have been Chicago's best overall win of the year with contributions from all three phases. Austin Booker made two big plays for the Bears, blocking a field goal in the fourth quarter and tipping the ball, which led to an interception by James Lynch.

Defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons also had an amazing debut for the Bears with two tackles for loss, including a sack as well as recording a quarterback hit and six total tackles. His presence also elevated Montez Sweat, who he was teammates with at Mississippi State. Sweat had his best game of the season by far with three quarterback hits and a sack.

Offensively, Chicago could not be denied in the red zone, scoring on all four of their red zone trips, including three rushing touchdowns from running back D'Andre Swift. Quarterback Caleb Williams had his third game of the season with completion percentage greater than 70 yards. He also become the first quarterback to throw for over 200 yards against Seattle.

Still, there are things to improve on. Their secondary has been dealing with injuries, including being without their top cornerback in coverage, Malik Muhammad. However, the biggest point of improvement is reducing their penalties. Chicago had 10 penalties in this game, making this their fifth game of the season with at least eight penalties. The Bears were able to overcome these penalties, but they could be even better if they were not penalized so much.

Anthony Richardson trade possibilities: Some teams should be interested in Colts quarterback

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Baltimore Ravens are in desperate need of a quarterback. Lamar Jackson (thumb) and Tyler Huntley (toe) left their Week 8 game, leaving Joe Fagnano as their only option. They signed Austin Reed to their practice squad on Monday, Nov. 2.

If the Ravens (4-4) want to remain in the AFC playoff hunt, they will likely need to make a move soon.

Former Indianapolis Colts quarterback and ESPN football analyst Dan Orlovsky has a suggestion: Colts QB Anthony Richardson.

Richardson made his first appearance of the season in the Colts' Week 8 blowout loss to the Jacksonville Jaguars, going 2-of-2 for 8 yards passing, with 4 rushing yards.

The Colts have shown willingness to trade team leaders, so the 2023 No. 4 overall NFL Draft pick could be on the block. He has appeared in 18 games, just three in the past 1 1/2 seasons. Also, the Colts could hand him the reins if Daniel Jones continues to struggle.

"The Ravens should call to inquire about Richardson for the hard-selling Colts. In this scenario, Baltimore would get the rushing element of Jackson back to work with (offensive coordinator Declan) Doyle. At worst, it's a low-risk move that won't cost the Ravens much for a half-season evaluation. At best, Richardson develops into a better long-term backup for Jackson."

"The Colts are blowing it up. They already traded away Nelson and DeForest Buckner, and Jonathan Taylor seems like he could be next if the price is right. If Indy is willing to move that caliber of player, trading Anthony Richardson for any sort of return should be on the table even after benching Daniel Jones late in the Week 8 loss to Jacksonville."

"Having Richardson in the Vikings' quarterback room would add competition for whoever Minnesota brings in during the offseason — either via trade, free agency or the 2027 NFL Draft — and raise the floor for the future of the position."

Joel A. Erickson and Nathan Brown cover the Colts all season. Get more coverage on IndyStarTV and with the Colts Insider newsletter.

Juventus CEO wants ‘more Italian identity’ within the squad

📅 Nov 03, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Juventus CEO Giovanni Carnevali believes the squad needs a "more Italian identity" if they are to challenge for major trophies again.

The Bianconeri have not won Serie A since 2019-20 after winning nine consecutive Scudetti from 2011-12 onwards.

Last season, following their early struggles under Igor Tudor, Luciano Spalletti's side failed to qualify for the Champions League after finishing sixth.

Juve have been inconsistent in the league this term, winning 17 points from 10 games to sit seventh, though they picked up a vital victory over Napoli on Sunday.

In their home win over Napoli, the only Italian player to start for Juve was goalkeeper Guglielmo Vicario, who is on loan from Tottenham.

Federico Gatti and Andrea Cambiaso both came off the bench, while Jeff Ekhator, Carlo Pinsoglio and Daniele Rugani were all unused subtitutes.

Carnevali was appointed in June this year to replace Damien Comolli, and the Juve CEO believes investing in youth players can help the club return to their former ways.

"We need more Italian identity within our club, and we need to develop young players who can one day make it into the Juventus first team," he said via Corriere della Sera.

— JuventusFC  (@juventusfcen) November 3, 2026

"We need to rediscover our enthusiasm and sense of belonging. We'll have to put in the effort, take things seriously, and have a strong desire to do things; we need clear ideas and to know where we want to go.

"I know really well that results over the last few years have not been positive. You must be good at overcoming difficult moments, and we can only do it together.

"We don't expect to achieve anything extraordinary overnight. We must comply with UEFA regulations. Rebuilding a great club takes time."
